2. **Relic Resonance Tracking**: Energy expended (`shield_power`, `sword_edge`) updates in persistent memory (`player_state_t`).
3. **Moral Sway Evaluation**:
   - Choosing to spare routed enemy captains, warding retreating militia, or using non-lethal shield stagger tilts sway toward **Virtue (+)**.
   - Executing fleeing enemies with abyssal severance or utilizing forbidden void arts tilts sway toward **Malice (-)**.
   - Moral sway unlocks distinct strategic faction options across the Kingdom of Sacyra.

== Chapter 33: The Twin Relics, Combat Stances & Moral Sway

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/relics_and_combat.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This document specifies the core ludological mechanics of *Tenebris and Lux* in the **Kingdom of Sacyra**
and **The Dreamscape**: the twin primordial relics (**The Shield of Lux** and **The Sword of Tenebris**),
the dynamic combat stance state machine, the moral sway continuum (`[-100, +100]`), and the tactical action
skills: **Defend** (Lux Barrio), **Attack** (Cuttatio Tenebris), and **Special** (Basic Magic, Basic Ward,
Basic Charm, and Duo-Loca Bashin n Slash).

== 1. Primordial Neutrality & Relic Mechanics

Neither relic is inherently good nor evil. They are manifestations of cosmic apex forces whose morality is
dictated solely by the intentions and choices of their wielders—canonically **Elijah Ro-enheart** and
**Katrina Jaren**, or a customizable bearer chosen by the player:

[cols="1,2,2", options="header"]
|===
| Relic | The Shield of Lux | The Sword of Tenebris
| **Aspect** | Apex Radiance & Cohesion | Apex Entropy & Dissolution
| **Energy Gauge** | `shield_power` ($0 \dots 100$) | `sword_edge` ($0 \dots 100$)
| **Canonical Bearer** | **Elijah Ro-enheart** (Vanguard Defense) | **Katrina Jaren** (Agile Severance)
| **Grid Role** | Zone-of-Control denial, sanctuary aura, ally protection | High-mobility flanking, armor penetration, mist evasion
| **Cinematic Action** | Timed kinetic blocks, shield-bashes, radiant shockwaves | Rapid dash strikes, armor severance, twilight blade waves
| **Resource Dynamics** | Regenerates on guard; absorbs incoming assault | Consumes edge on strikes; hones outside combat
|===

== 2. The Combat Stance State Machine

The battle posture shifts dynamically across four discrete combat stances:

[source,text]
----
                   ┌───────────────────┐
                   │  STANCE_NEUTRAL   │
                   └─────────┬─────────┘
                             │
         ┌───────────────────┼───────────────────┐
         ▼                   ▼                   ▼
┌─────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────┐
│ STANCE_WARDING  │ │ STANCE_STRIKING │ │STANCE_EQUILIBRIUM
│ (Shield Guard)  │ │ (Sword Drawn)   │ │ (Twin Harmony)  │
└─────────────────┘ └─────────────────┘ └─────────────────┘
----

1. **`STANCE_NEUTRAL`**: Relics held at waist. Balanced baseline.
   - Standard movement on the tactical grid; baseline attack parameters.
2. **`STANCE_WARDING`**: Shield raised in protective vanguard high-guard.
   - Grid Effect: Increases allied defense within adjacent tiles by $+25\%$.
   - Combat Effect: Boosts Shield power recovery ($+15$), increases stagger damage against attacking foes ($25$ HP), and wards against incoming status curses.
3. **`STANCE_STRIKING`**: Greatsword held in aggressive two-handed assault posture.
   - Grid Effect: Increases unit movement by $+1$ tile and grants armor-piercing flank bonuses.
   - Combat Effect: Increases strike damage from $30$ to $45$ (or base $+15$ bonus); reduces edge cost from $12$ to $8$ points.
4. **`STANCE_EQUILIBRIUM`**: Harmonized twin-relic synchronization.
   - Grid Effect: Immunity to positioning debuffs and void mire terrain penalties.
   - Combat Effect: Simultaneously restores $+15$ Shield power and $+15$ Sword edge; parries incoming strikes while counter-attacking for $20$ damage; nudges moral sway toward center ($0$).

== 3. The Moral Sway Continuum

The moral trajectory of Sacyra's champions is tracked along a continuous bounded integer axis:

[source,text]
----
[ -100: Sovereign of Ruin ] ◄──── [ 0: Equilibrium ] ────► [ +100: Saint of Mercy ]
----

* **Virtue & Mercy ($+1 \dots +100$)**:
  - Gained by shielding fleeing militia, sparing defeated enemy officers, resolving noble-court corruption through justice, and protecting the innocent with the Shield of Lux.
  - Unlocks royal alliances, Knight Commander endorsements, and sanctuary miracles.
* **Tyranny & Ruin ($-1 \dots -100$)**:
  - Accumulated through ruthless executions, collateral damage in villages, using scorched-earth radiance to blind subjects, or giving in to the void's bloodlust.
  - Unlocks forbidden void arts, mercenary enlistments, and intimidation tactics.
* **Equilibrium ($0$)**:
  - Maintained by balancing the blade and the aegis; recognizing that light without shadow is a calcified wasteland, and shadow without light is an endless void.
  - Unlocks the ultimate secret synthesis arts of ancient Sacyra.

== 4. Tactical Actions & Combat Skills

In the revised, authentic combat framework, basic actions expand into dedicated martial and arcane skills:

=== 4.1. Defend (`defend` / `ward`)
* **Standard Defend**: Braces behind the Shield of Lux, regenerating $+10 \dots +15$ shield power and mitigating incoming damage.
* **Basic Skill: `Lux Barrio` (`barrio`)**:
  - Manifests a radiant, geometric barrier of pure light.
  - **Effect**: Blocks incoming attack and reduces subsequent incoming damage by **$25\%$** for a rolled turn duration.
  - **Duration Roll**: Deterministically rolls for **$2 \dots 5$ turns** (`2 + (turns % 4)`).
  - **Resonance**: Saturates Shield of Lux by $+20$ points (capped at $100$) and awards $+3$ moral virtue.

=== 4.2. Attack (`attack` / `strike`)
* **Standard Attack**: Strikes the target enemy using the Sword of Tenebris, consuming edge points and dealing physical damage (`(ATK * 2) - DEF`).
* **Basic Skill: `Cuttatio Tenebris` (`cuttatio`)**:
  - A sweeping abyssal crescent slash infused with dark mist.
  - **Direct Damage**: Deals full primary severance damage to the selected target.
  - **Splash Damage**: Deals **$20\%$ splash damage** to surrounding and flanking foes (`threat_surrounding_hp`).
  - **Edge Dynamic**: Consumes $8 \dots 12$ edge points.

=== 4.3. Special (`special` / `balance`)
Replaces balance with a four-fold technique tree honoring the twin relics and arcane arts:

1. **`Basic Magic` (`magic`)**:
   - Neutral elemental magic damage attacking enemy Magic Defense (`(MAT * 2) - MDF`).
   - Costs $10$ MP. Pure, unaligned cosmic energy.
2. **`Basic Ward` (`ward` under special)**:
   - Magic-based defend weaving an arcane barrier.
   - Restores $+15$ Shield power and staggers incoming assault without relying purely on physical bracing.
   - Costs $6$ MP.
3. **`Basic Charm` (`charm`)**:
   - Enhances astral focus, granting **$+20\%$ Magic Attack (MAT)** for **$1 \dots 3$ turns** (`1 + (turns % 3)`).
   - Costs $8$ MP.
4. **`Duo-Loca Bashin n Slash` (`duo`)**:
   - The signature dual-relic strike uniting the Shield of Lux and Sword of Tenebris.
   - **Adaptive Damage**: Intelligently evaluates whether the player's Magic Attack or Physical Attack is superior, or whichever enemy defense (DEF vs MDF) is lower, channeling into the foe's weakest resistance:
     * If enemy MDF < DEF or MAT > ATK: executes as **Magical Twilight Slash**.
     * Otherwise: executes as **Physical Radiant Bash**.
   - **Relic Harmony**: Simultaneously restores $+15$ Shield power and $+15$ Sword edge, while nudging moral sway toward absolute cosmic equilibrium ($0$).
   - Costs $12$ MP.

=== 4.4. Hero Customization & Protagonist Lore
* While **Elijah Ro-enheart** and **Katrina Jaren** remain the canonical dual protagonists representing the Shield of Lux and Sword of Tenebris, the player can dynamically designate their own hero identity using the `name <chosen_name>` command.
* All narrative chronicle logs, character severity tags, and telemetry traces adapt cleanly to the bearer's active name.

== 5. The Metaphysical Law of Tempering & Dissipation

The interaction between the Shield of Lux and the Sword of Tenebris is governed by the ancient law established during the 7-Year Great War:

* **Lux as the Singular Tempering Agent:** Raw Tenebris is entropic and severing; left unchecked, it erodes both target and wielder. Lux is the only force capable of containing and tempering Tenebris, providing structural cohesion so that void strikes can be directed safely.
* **The Fragility of Radiance (Dissipation):** While Lux tempers Tenebris, it requires emotional and ethical stability. Any sudden surge of terror, malice, or panic causes Lux to **dissipate immediately**, collapsing protective barriers and leaving Tenebris unbridled.
* **Ludonarrative Impact:** In combat, executing dark commands with negative moral sway degrades Lux Barrio duration rolls, while maintaining Equilibrium (`0` sway) maximizes barrier resilience and tandem skill execution.

== 6. The Relic Embellishment System

Throughout the campaign, the Shield of Lux and Sword of Tenebris are actively embellished through quest outcomes, moral choices, and auxiliary relics:

1. **Eric's Test of Honesty (Map 028) $\to$ The Ring of Fire:**
   - Donating rent to Eric rewards the **Ring of Fire**, imbuing strikes and barriers with flame-weave properties, connecting directly to the LeTBS *Burning Tile Spread* tactical state.
2. **Monica's Offering (Maps 027/029) $\to$ The Book of Monsters:**
   - Sponsoring an offering to **Lyri, Goddess of Peace**, attunes the relics to arcane resonance and awards the **Book of Monsters**, unlocking advanced MDF-targeting formulas.
3. **John the Thief vs. St. Baron (Maps 030/031):**
   - Aiding John enhances critical severance and stealth penetration; upholding feudal property law reinforces kinetic poise and bastion authority.
4. **Armory Relics (Medusa's Sword & Heart Shield):**
   - Optional material infusions granting petrification stasis or passive HP vitality warding.

== 7. Relic Profiles & Ending Correlation

The final state of relic embellishments along the `[-100, +100]` Moral Sway continuum determines which of the five ending routes resolves the campaign:
* **Route A (Canonical Harmony):** Balanced embellishments, Stance of Equilibrium, True Ending.
* **Route B (Benevolent Stasis):** Pure Lux embellishment, `+100` sway, continental comatose barrier.
* **Route C (Calcified Tyrant):** Lux embellishment, `-100` sway, inquisitorial solar tyranny.
* **Route D (Abyssal Usurper):** Pure Tenebris embellishment, `-100` sway, Dark Sovereign ascension.
* **Route E (Secret Chronicler):** Custom bearer, 100% relic synthesis, planar tear healed.


== Chapter 34: Campaign Architecture, The King's Curse & Multi-Ending Specification

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/campaign_structure_and_endings.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview & Ludological Philosophy

This specification articulates the structural macro-architecture of the campaign in *Tenebris and Lux*,
including the two-half campaign progression, the metaphysical etiology of "The King's Curse," the midpoint
narrative fork, player agency over lore discovery, and the complete five-route ending matrix. Built upon
the premise of ludonarrative consent, the engine allows casual players to experience a coherent linear
tactical campaign, while reserving the mythological truth for players who actively engage with nocturnal
Dreamscape trials.

== 1. The Two-Half Campaign Structure

The overarching campaign is organized into two distinct dramatic and mechanical halves:

[source,text]
----
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   ACT I: THE SACYRAN SUCCESSION CRISIS                 │
│  • Setting: Dio Village, Whispering Woods, Capital Outposts            │
│  • Antagonist Focus: King Jihad & Corrupt Royal Court                  │
│  • Themes: Provincial neglect, family mysteries, state censorship     │
│  • Climax: The Festival of Heroes & Siege of Sacyra Castle             │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                   MIDPOINT FORK: THE KING'S CURSE                      │
│  • Defeat of King Jihad reveals possession by The Dark Lord's remnant  │
│  • Structural branching based on nocturnal Dream Compass completion    │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                   ACT II: THE COSMIC RECKONING                         │
│  • Setting: Sacyra Marches, Abyssal Vaults, The Dreamscape Core        │
│  • Antagonist Focus: The Dark Lord (Usuru's Ancient Void Essence)      │
│  • Themes: Severance vs. Cohesion, Atonement, Planar Reconciliation   │
│  • Climax: Confrontation at the Cosmic Threshold & Ending Resolution   │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
----

=== 1.1. Act I: Stopping King Jihad (The Sovereign of Ambition)
Act I centers on terrestrial, socio-political conflict. Operating from Dio Village through the Whispering
Woods and Capital marches, the party uncovers King Jihad's treasonous suppression of border alerts. The
act culminates during the **Festival of Heroes** at Sacyra Castle, where King Jihad is confronted and defeated.

=== 1.2. The Etiology of "The King's Curse" & Royal Possession
Court superstition long spoke of a hereditary madness afflicting monarchs of Sacyra. Forensic narrative
architecture reveals that "The King's Curse" is not a demonic hex, but an active **metaphysical hazard**:

* **The Open Planar Wound:** When eleven-year-old Usuru shattered the First King's stasis projection ritual
  3,000 years ago, a jagged planar rift tore open directly beneath the dias of the **High Throne**.
* **Psychic Backwash:** For three millennia, reigning monarchs sitting upon the High Throne were subjected
  to perpetual psychic shear and planar backwash from across the veil.
* **Jihad's Fatal Gambit:** Seeking to usurp the Dark Lord's void power during the thinned veil of the
  Festival of Heroes, King Jihad intentionally opened the conduit beneath his throne. Because the ethereal
  medium mirrors mortal intent, Jihad's megalomania inverted the flow, allowing a dormant psychic remnant
  of Usuru (**The Dark Lord**) to possess his body.

== 2. The Midpoint Fork: Neutral Path vs. True Ending Path

Defeating King Jihad reveals the void possession, forcing the narrative into its primary structural fork:

[source,text]
----
                         ACT I CLIMAX: DEFEAT OF KING JIHAD
                   Possession Revealed via "The King's Curse"
                                       │
            ┌──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┐
            ▼                                                     ▼
   THE NEUTRAL PATH (Basic Completion)                   THE TRUE ENDING PATH (100% Synthesis)
   • Dreamscape side quests missed/skipped.              • Nocturnal Dream Compass quests completed.
   • Treats possession as an external demon hex.         • Recognizes the severed ritual's energy signature.
   • Marches to slay or supplant The Dark Lord.          • Uncovers the First King's seal & Usuru's grief.
            │                                                     │
     ┌──────┴──────┐                                       ┌──────┴──────┐
     ▼             ▼                                       ▼             ▼
"Good" Ending  "Bad" Ending                        Canonical Harmony  Secret Chronicler
(Heroic Myth)  (Tyrant/Usurper)                    (Elijah + Katrina) (Custom Bearer)
----

=== 2.1. Why Basic Completion Is the Neutral Ending
By design, any player who simply plays the main story linearly from start to finish will reach the end
of the campaign. However, skipping the missable nocturnal **Dream Compass** trials produces the **Neutral Ending**:

* The player defeats King Jihad and destroys The Dark Lord's physical manifestation.
* The kingdom is saved in the present, but the **First King remains permanently marooned** in The Dreamscape.
* Usuru's ancient wound of betrayal is left unhealed, and the systemic neglect between the Capital and frontier
  provinces remains unchanged, ensuring the cycle will repeat.

=== 2.2. Player Consent to Know the Deeper Lore
Video games are distinguished by interactive agency. Forcing forty minutes of historical exposition down the
player's throat violates ludic pacing. By gating the full mythological truth behind optional, nocturnal
Dreamscape trials, the game honors the player's agency: players who desire a fast-paced tactical campaign
can enjoy a satisfying heroic conclusion, while players who actively investigate the mystery are rewarded
with the full mythological reality.

=== 2.3. Why the True Ending Is "The Ugly Truth"
The canonical True Ending is neither a simplistic fairy-tale triumph nor a dark nihilistic collapse. It is
**"The Ugly Truth"** that dismantles the self-serving propaganda of both sides:

* It exposes that Prince Mi-ren's loving preservation created a catastrophic institutional lie that deceived
  a continent for six festivals.
* It acknowledges that Usuru's childhood outrage was entirely valid before his unguided fury curdled into
  monstrous destruction.
* It reveals that Sacyra's three millennia of golden peace were bought at the cost of deliberate neglect
  toward frontier provinces like Libira and Dio Village.

== 3. Relic Embellishment & The Five-Route Ending Matrix

Throughout both acts, the player's choices, side quests, and stance preferences actively **embellish**
the Shield of Lux and Sword of Tenebris along the `[-100, +100]` **Moral Sway** continuum:

[cols="1,2,2,3", options="header"]
|===
| Ending Route | Playable Requirement | Relic Embellishment & Moral Sway Profile | Narrative & Metaphysical Resolution

| **Route A: The Canonical Harmony** _(True Equilibrium Ending)_
| Complete the campaign playing through **both** Elijah's and Katrina's perspectives; finish all Dreamscape trials.
| Balanced embellishments; Moral Sway harmonized at Equilibrium (`0`); master the Stance of Equilibrium (*Duo-Loca Bashin n Slash*).
| Exposes King Jihad's corruption; severs Usuru's abyssal tether while the Shield absorbs the fallout without dissipating; grants Usuru the reconciliation Mi-ren sought 3,000 years ago; releases the First King's soul to final rest.

| **Route B: The Benevolent Stasis** _(Neutral-Good / The Mi-ren Fallacy)_
| High Lux focus with pure preservation choices; reject Tenebris severance; ignore Dreamscape truth.
| Pure Lux barrier embellishments; Moral Sway pinned at `+100`.
| Terrified of void destruction, the wielder repeats Mi-ren's ancient error on a continental scale, sealing Sacyra inside a comatose golden barrier where nothing can harm—and no living being can grow.

| **Route C: The Calcified Tyrant** _(Neutral-Bad / Corrupted Order)_
| High Lux embellishments paired with authoritarian, merciless choices; negative moral sway (`-100`).
| Petrifying solar embellishments; high stasis lockdown.
| Overthrows King Jihad only to establish an inquisitorial police state, using blinding radiance to enforce absolute compliance and extinguish personal dissent.

| **Route D: The Abyssal Usurper** _(Neutral-Bad / The Cycle of Usuru)_
| High Tenebris focus with ruthless malice (`-100`); endorse theft and indiscriminate executions.
| Pure Tenebris severance embellishments; Lux completely dissipated by wrath.
| Agreeing that Sacyra's court is irredeemably hypocritical, the wielder slays King Jihad, absorbs The Dark Lord's void essence, razes the Capital, and ascends the High Throne as the new Dark Sovereign.

| **Route E: The Secret Chronicler Ending** _(Custom Bearer 100% Route)_
| Complete the campaign as `<chosen_name>`; perform the "double work" across both protagonists' questlines; achieve $100\%$ Dreamscape nocturnal trials.
| Full mastery of all relic embellishments, Magic vs. Science synthesis, and complete planar harmony.
| The custom bearer bridges the Magic vs. Science schism, dissolves royal secrecy, establishes an egalitarian relay outpost network for frontier settlements, and permanently mends the planar wound in the Room of Sages.
|===

== 4. Systemic Invariants & Engine Triggers

All ending routes and campaign forks are evaluated through deterministic engine invariants in Layer 1:

1. **`player_state_t` Integrity:** The moral sway value (`int16_t moral_sway`) is clamped strictly
   within `[-100, +100]` via `math_clamp_int`.
2. **Dream Compass Nocturnal Flag:** Entry into `LOC_THE_DREAMSCAPE` requires `hour >= 20 || hour <= 4`
   from the Layer 0 monotonic clock.
3. **Equilibrium State Check:** Unlocking Route A requires `abs(moral_sway) <= 15` and both
   `shield_power >= 80` and `sword_edge >= 80` during the final turn sequence.

== Chapter 35: The Dreamscape & Temporal Architecture

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/dreamscape_and_temporal_architecture.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview & Engine Integration

This document specifies the technical, mechanical, and temporal architecture of **The Dreamscape**
in *Tenebris and Lux*. Grounded in the recovered 2017–2018 RPG Maker MV demo baseline (Maps 006, 008,
011, 041, 042) and integrated into the ISO C11 engine architecture, it formalizes the planar opening
sequence with Chrono the Sage, the terrestrial demo gating, the evolution of **Grandma's Watch** into
**The Dream Compass**, and nocturnal event-driven temporal phasing.

== 1. Engine Architecture & Topological Verification

The Dreamscape is not an ephemeral cutscene or narrative metaphor; it is an active, fully integrated
subsystem registered within the Layer 1 simulation engine:

=== 1.1. Explicit Province Registration
The plane is codified as an explicit topological enumeration in `include/sim/sim_types.h`:

[source,c]
----
typedef enum {
    LOC_THE_DREAMSCAPE    = 0,  /* Astral origin, ancestral archive, planar rift */
    LOC_DIO_VILLAGE       = 1,  /* Pastoral woodland hamlet; Grandma's hearth    */
    LOC_WHISPERING_WOODS  = 2,  /* Outlying frontier timberlands; beast paths    */
    LOC_SACYRA_MARCHES    = 3,  /* Fortified royal road to the capital citadel   */
    LOC_COUNT             = 4
} location_id_t;
----

=== 1.2. Tactical LeTBS Battlefields
The Dreamscape encompasses five dedicated grid environments extracted from the historical archives:

* **Map 006 (Chrono's Archive / The Hall of Sages):** Archetype stone pedestals, floating crystal
  columns, and ancestral book pedestals.
* **Map 008 (The Astral Crucible):** Tutorial grid where the bearer tests `Lux Barrio` and
  `Cuttatio Tenebris` against encroaching void scouts.
* **Map 011 (The Shifting Leyline Bridges):** Dynamic tactical grid featuring floating platforms
  and crystal floor switches that alter terrain heights.
* **Maps 041 & 042 (The Deep Astral Depths):** High-level nocturnally phased battlefields featuring
  astral leyline conduits that replenish MP while inflicting planar shear.

=== 1.3. Parameterized Combatants: The Dream Eaters
The Dreamscape is populated by **Dream Eaters**—parasitic entities dispatched across the thinning veil
by The Dark Lord to devour ancestral records prior to the Festival of Heroes:

[source,text]
----
Threat Profile: Dream Eater
├── Threat HP: 70
├── Physical Defense (DEF): 11
├── Arcane Defense (MDF): 8
├── Flank Vigor: 70
└── Tactical AI: Rushes fragile casters; vulnerable to radiant deflection (Lux Barrio).
----

=== 1.4. Bidirectional Topology
The spatial graph connects seamlessly between the metaphysical and physical planes:
* Northward: `Awaken` $\to$ `LOC_DIO_VILLAGE` (Grandma Magolia's Hearth).
* Southward: `Slumber Meditation` $\to$ `LOC_THE_DREAMSCAPE` (Requires Dream Compass nighttime flag).

== 2. Narrative Inception: The Astral Rite of Awakening

=== 2.1. Unformed Potential in the Astral Archive
The game opens not in a mundane tavern or military camp, but inside the timeless ether of **The Dreamscape**.
Because this plane houses 3,000 years of ancestral memory—and the marooned soul of the First King—having
**Chrono the Sage** guide the player through class archetype stones elevates character creation into
a canonical ritual of spiritual attunement.

=== 2.2. Tri-Perspective Adaptability
Opening in unformed ether allows the simulation to cleanly accommodate any of the three playable vantage
points—**Elijah Ro-enheart**, **Lady Katrina Jaren**, or a custom-named bearer (`<chosen_name>`)—before
their identity crystallizes into physical form.

=== 2.3. The Ludonarrative Shock of "AWAKEN!"
After proving tactical mastery against Dream Eaters in the abstract void, Chrono banishes the player
with an urgent command: _"AWAKEN!"_. The immediate cut to Grandma Magolia's crackling birch hearth in
Dio Village shocks the player into physical reality, establishing a stark contrast between cosmic
destiny and human vulnerability.

== 3. Terrestrial Gating & The Dream Compass

=== 3.1. Chapter 1 Demo Gating as a Terrestrial Anchor
Throughout Chapter 1 (the entire demo narrative), the player is strictly locked out of returning to
The Dreamscape. This design rule prevents premature cosmic dilution, forcing the player to invest
emotionally in the grounded human stakes of Dio Village:
* Grandma Magolia's failing health and maternal wisdom.
* Eric the Lumberjack's economic ruin from monster-infested logging trails.
* John the Thief's moral crusade against the predatory St. Baron.
* Monica the Mystic's grief beside the totem of fallen border soldiers.

=== 3.2. From Grandma's Watch to The Dream Compass
In the opening sequence, the player retrieves **Grandma's Watch** from the upstairs chest in Dio Village
(Map 020). Mechanically, this initializes the Layer 0 `time_utils` 24-hour monotonic clock:

[source,text]
----
Layer 0 Monotonic Clock Subsystem
├── Seconds / Minutes
├── Hours (00:00 to 23:59)
├── Weekdays (1: Sol, 2: Luna, 3: Ignis, 4: Aqua, 5: Terra, 6: Ventus, 7: Aether)
├── Weeks / Months
└── Calendar Countdown to The Festival of Heroes
----

Upon concluding the Chapter 1 demo introduction, Grandma's Watch undergoes its first metaphysical
transmutation, evolving into **The Dream Compass**.

=== 3.3. Nocturnal Synchronization & Scarcity
The Dream Compass acts as a dimensional resonant tuning fork with strict activation criteria:
* **Nighttime Window Only:** The Dream Compass functions exclusively during nocturnal hours
  (`20:00 <= hour || hour <= 04:00`). During daylight hours, the compass needle spins aimlessly.
* **Event-Driven Astral Windows:** Nocturnal side quests and main story trials in The Dreamscape
  manifest only during specific astrological alignments and calendar milestones.
* **Scarcity and Sacred Weight:** By restricting access to nocturnal windows, The Dreamscape is
  prevented from becoming a trivial grinding hub, preserving its sacred mystery and directly linking
  quest availability to the monotonic clock's relentless countdown toward the Festival of Heroes.

== Chapter 36: World Topography, Provinces & Battlefield Terrain

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/world_topography.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This document specifies the authentic spatial geography, province layouts, environmental encounters,
and tactical grid battlefields governing the **Kingdom of Sacyra** (pronounced *"saw-kee-ruh"*, `/sɔːˈkiːrə/`)
and **The Dreamscape**, grounded in the recovered 2017–2018 demo baseline.
While **The Dark Lord** looms as the overarching existential threat in the background ("A Plot"),
the narrative begins within the ethereal trials of **The Dreamscape** before awakening into the pastoral
hamlet of **Dio Village**. Players may guide the canonical champions (**Elijah Ro-enheart** and
**Katrina Jaren**) or customize their chosen bearer's designation.

== 1. Spatial Structure: The Realm Topology

The campaign begins in the trial realm of slumber and transitions into the physical topology of Sacyra:

[source,text]
----
┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
│          The Sacyran Marches          │
│  (Royal Frontier towards the Capital) │
└───────────────────▲───────────────────┘
                    │ East / North
┌───────────────────┴───────────────────┐
│           Whispering Woods            │
│  (Ancient Forest Surrounding Hamlet)  │
└───────────────────▲───────────────────┘
                    │ North / South
┌───────────────────┴───────────────────┐
│              Dio Village              │
│  (Grandma Magolia's Hearth & Timber)  │
└───────────────────▲───────────────────┘
                    │ Awaken / North
┌───────────────────┴───────────────────┐
│            The Dreamscape             │
│   (Trial Realm & Dream Eater Combat)  │
└───────────────────────────────────────┘
----

=== 1. The Dreamscape (`LOC_THE_DREAMSCAPE`)
* **Role**: The ethereal realm of ancient trials guided by Chrono where the illusion of the dream shatters.
* **Environment**: Floating crystal archways, shifting astral mist, and mechanism trial chambers.
* **Significance**: The hero awakens here to learn the fundamentals of combat and relic resonance.
  Dream Eaters gather under the distant influence of **The Dark Lord**.
* **Encounter**: **Dream Eater** ($70$ Threat HP, $11$ DEF, $8$ MDF, $70$ Flank Vigor).
* **Grid Battlefield**: Astral tiles, floating platforms, and ethereal leylines granting MP regeneration.
* **Connections**: North / Awaken $\to$ Dio Village.

=== 2. Dio Village (`LOC_DIO_VILLAGE`)
* **Role**: Pastoral ancestral hamlet in Sacyra; the true awakening point of the physical journey.
* **Environment**: Cozy stone cottages with thatched roofs, Grandma Magolia's welcoming hearth,
  Eric the lumberjack's timber yard, and the local general trading post.
* **Significance**: Here the hero recovers Grandma's Watch and the *Compass of Morality*, encountering
  village folk before venturing into the perilous wilds.
* **Threat Vigor**: $0$ (Sanctified village sanctuary; recovery, dialogue, and lore exchange).
* **Connections**: South $\to$ The Dreamscape (slumber meditation); North $\to$ Whispering Woods.

=== 3. Whispering Woods (`LOC_WHISPERING_WOODS`)
* **Role**: Dense ancient forest enveloping Dio Village, separating the hamlet from the kingdom's interior.
* **Environment**: Towering ironwood boughs, rustling autumn canopy, and hidden woodland trails.
* **Encounter**: **Umbral Wolf** ($85$ Threat HP, $14$ DEF, $10$ MDF, $60$ Flank Vigor).
* **Tactical Grid Elements**:
  - Forest thickets providing $+20\%$ evasion against ranged physical assault.
  - Tree root chokepoints where *Lux Barrio* shields flanking allies from pack ambushes.
* **Connections**: South $\to$ Dio Village; East / North $\to$ The Sacyran Marches.

=== 4. The Sacyran Marches (`LOC_SACYRA_MARCHES`)
* **Role**: The windswept frontier marches leading toward the Royal Court of King Jihad and the Capital.
* **Environment**: Craggy hills, fortified signal towers, and military encampments.
* **Encounter**: **Shadow Scout & Vanguard Patrol** ($110$ Threat HP, $18$ DEF, $14$ MDF, $80$ Flank Vigor).
* **Tactical Grid Elements**:
  - Trenches, palisades, and elevated ramparts where *Cuttatio Tenebris* cleaves through enemy formations.
  - Broad fields allowing full maneuverability of twin-relic equilibrium strikes.
* **Connections**: West / South $\to$ Whispering Woods.

== 2. The Overarching Menace: The Dark Lord

In the authentic 2017–2018 canonical script, the ultimate antagonist is designated strictly as **The Dark Lord**:
* He is never named "Malakor" or burdened with synthetic titles in canon.
* The Dark Lord represents the primordial threat looming in the distance ("The A Plot"), while regional
  intrigues—such as King Jihad's political maneuvers, Noble House power struggles, and the Adventurers' Guild
  missions—unfold on the active stage.
* The wielder's mastery of the Shield of Lux and Sword of Tenebris prepares them for this looming confrontation
  without rushing directly into the final battle.

== Chapter 37: Continuous Invariant Testing Philosophy

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/game_testing.adoc`

[abstract]
== Abstract

In classical software engineering, testing focuses primarily on stateless input/output validation,
API schemas, and unit function assertions (`assert(f(x) == y)`). In game development, testing
requires an entirely distinct paradigm: verifying continuous state continuity, economy balance,
softlock immunity, deterministic replay parity, and strict 60 FPS frame-budget headroom.
This document outlines the testing philosophy governing *Tenebris and Lux*.

== 1. The Fundamental Divergence

[cols="1,2,2", options="header"]
|===
| Dimension | Traditional Software Testing | Game Development Testing
| **State Nature** | Ephemeral, localized, often stateless | Continuous, cumulative, multi-turn state machine
| **Failure Modes** | Null pointer exceptions, HTTP 500s, syntax errors | Softlocks, economy collapse, degenerate gameplay loops, visual desync
| **Correctness Metric** | Does output match expected schema? | Does the game feel right? Is the state bounded, balanced, and unexploitable?
| **Input Cadence** | Controlled, structured API payloads | Unbounded, chaotic human keystrokes, rapid button mash, simultaneous events
| **Time Budget** | Milliseconds/seconds per request | 16.6 milliseconds total frame budget (60 FPS) with microseconds for simulation
|===

== 2. Core Pillars of Game Development Testing

=== Pillar 1: Dynamic State Invariants & Bounded Continuity

In a game like *Tenebris and Lux*, state variables (`shield_power`, `sword_edge`, `moral_sway`, `threat_hp`)
are constantly mutated across hundreds of player turns. A single off-by-one error or uncapped addition
will cause state drift, resulting in overflow, negative health zombies, or invulnerability exploits.

* **Invariant Axioms**:
  - `0 <= state.shield_power <= 100` must hold after every single action turn.
  - `0 <= state.sword_edge <= 100` must hold even when spamming strikes at zero energy.
  - `-100 <= state.moral_sway <= +100` must never overflow into undefined integers.
* **Diagnostic Tripwires**: The game engine provides `game_audit_state()`, a self-healing diagnostic
  routine that asserts all invariants simultaneously and outputs a detailed diagnostic manifest
  without crashing the game process.

=== Pillar 2: Deterministic Replay ("Golden Master Replay")

Games are emergent systems. If two players—or an automated CI runner—replay the exact same sequence of
commands from the same seed, the end state must be **100% bitwise identical**:

[source,c]
----
// Golden Master Replay Assertion
TEST_ASSERT(run1.shield_power == run2.shield_power, "Deterministic shield");
TEST_ASSERT(run1.sword_edge   == run2.sword_edge,   "Deterministic sword");
TEST_ASSERT(run1.moral_sway   == run2.moral_sway,   "Deterministic sway");
TEST_ASSERT(run1.threat_hp    == run2.threat_hp,    "Deterministic threat");
----

This ensures that gameplay balance, combat resolution, and narrative branching do not drift across
compiler optimizations, operating systems, or refactored engine code.

=== Pillar 3: Softlock Immunity & Degenerate Play Detection

A **softlock** occurs when a player is technically still playing, but has no valid action forward:
- Running out of relic energy during an un-fleeable battle.
- Getting trapped behind a one-way gate with no exit command.
- Inputting malformed or rapid chaotic strings that cause the parser to lock up.

Our automated stress suite executes **1,000 rapid chaotic commands** (combining invalid strings,
rapid stance transitions, and empty tokens), verifying that the game engine rejects nonsense safely,
maintains continuous memory integrity, and never enters a deadlocked state.

=== Pillar 4: Economy & Combat Balance Invariance

Game testing must verify that no single player action is universally dominant (the "dominant strategy" flaw).
If a player can simply spam `ward` forever without consequence, or if `strike` renders all other actions
useless, the game design collapses:
- Warding increases shield power and moral virtue, but deals limited stagger damage.
- Striking inflicts decisive severance damage, but consumes finite sword edge.
- Balancing recovers both relics and parries attacks, but requires timing and resets extreme moral sway.

=== Pillar 5: Frame-Budget Latency Headroom

In standard software, a backend database query taking 50 ms is acceptable. In game development,
a game running at 60 FPS has **only 16.6 milliseconds** to:
1. Poll hardware events and window queues.
2. Execute game simulation logic.
3. Compute spatial audio and particle animations.
4. Render the scene graph to the backbuffer and blit to the display.

Simulation logic must therefore execute in **microseconds**.
In our automated benchmark (`test_gameplay_scenarios`), 10,000 action turns execute in **160 ms**
(**16.02 $\mu\text{s}$ per turn**), consuming less than **$0.1\%$** of a 60 FPS frame.

== Chapter 38: Game Design Curation & Triaging Matrix

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/demo_baseline/game_design_curation.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This document formalizes the architectural triage of the 2017–2018 RPG Maker MV
demo datasets. It separates generic RPG Maker Runtime Package (RTP) boilerplate
from temporary testing experiments and identifies the authentic, fleshed-out
canonical systems to be reconstructed in the native C11 game engine.

== 1. Triage Categorization Taxonomy

To prevent technical debt from legacy game engine defaults from contaminating
our custom C11 engine, all extracted game data is triaged into three strict
analytical categories:

[cols="1,2,3", options="header"]
|===
| Category | Definition | Architectural Policy
| **Tier 1: RTP Defaults & Boilerplate** | Stock RPG Maker MV assets, generic items, placeholder sounds, and default formulas. | **Discard / Re-architect**. Never port verbatim into C11.
| **Tier 2: Prototype & Testing Sketches** | Ad-hoc test maps, sandbox battlers, debug switches, and experimental scripts. | **Review & Evolve**. Extract mechanical concepts but redesign within clean strata.
| **Tier 3: Fleshed-Out Canonical Pillars** | Core story scripts, dual-protagonist dialogue dynamics, moral dilemma quests, LeTBS grid tactics, and key relics. | **Preserve & Reconstruct**. Faithfully implement as axiomatic invariants in C11.
|===

== 2. Tier 1: RPG Maker RTP Defaults (To Discard or Re-Architect)

[cols="2,2,3", options="header"]
|===
| Element | Historical Manifest | Why It Must Be Discarded / Replaced
| **Basic Consumables** | Potion (50G), Magic Water (100G), Dispel Herb (20G), Stimulant (50G) | Generic RTP recovery items. In C11, consumables must tie directly to Sacyran herbalism, relic alchemy, and stance restoration.
| **Generic Armaments** | Standard Sword (100G), Axe (100G), Cane (100G), Bow (100G), Shield (100G), Cloth (100G) | Default placeholder inventory without distinct tactical affinities or stance interactions.
| **Stock Sound Effects** | `Attack2`, `Buzzer1`, `Damage4`, `Collapse1-4`, `Shop1` | Generic MV sound library. C11 audio subsystem will support custom soundscapes.
| **Placeholder Bestiary** | Bat (`HP: 80`), Slime (`HP: 250`), Rabbit (`HP: 55`), Orc (`HP: 300`) | Standard MV demo monsters used for basic stat checks. Replaced by Sacyran fauna and Abyssal void aberrations.
| **Default Title Theme** | `Theme6` / `CrossedSwords` title screen graphic | Default MV asset packaging. Replaced by bespoke Sacyran coat of arms and twin relic artwork.
|===

== 3. Tier 2: Prototype & Testing Sketches (Concepts to Evolve)

[cols="2,2,3", options="header"]
|===
| Element | Historical Location | Tactical / Design Value to Evolve
| **Sandbox Test Maps** | `Map006 Test Areas`, `Map043 Event Map` | Used to test event triggers, movement paths, and sprite coordinates. Demonstrates intent for environmental puzzle mechanics.
| **Debug Enemies & Troops** | `Enemy 14 Test`, `Troop 10 Test`, `Enemy 32 Invincible Neutral` | Proved that neutral, invincible obstacles and destructible props function well on the tactical grid.
| **Explosive Chickens** | Fire Chicken (`Actor 13`), Bomb (`Skill 136`) | Humorous prototype mechanic demonstrating combat summons and dynamic decoy units on the tactical field.
| **Plugin Experiments** | `LeTBS/firestorm.json`, `LeTBS/test.json`, `QMaps/` | Initial experiments with Lecode's Tactical Battle System and Quasi Movement plugins. Proves grid-based TRPG was the true target genre.
|===

== 4. Tier 3: Fleshed-Out Canonical Pillars (Core Invariants to Reconstruct)

The following systems represent the authentic creative vision of *Tenebris and Lux*
and must be preserved and reconstructed in C11:

=== 1. Dual-Protagonist Dynamics & Narrative Branching
* **Elijah Ro-enheart (`\n[1]`)**: Son of vanished Commander Gareth Ro-enheart.
  Dutiful, protective vanguard bearer of the Shield of Lux. Dialogue reflects
  reverence for duty, family loyalty, and hesitation to violate laws.
* **Lady Katrina Jaren (`\n[2]`)**: Daughter of noble House Jaren escaping courtly
  politics to join the Adventurers' Guild. Agile outrider wielder of the Sword of
  Tenebris. Dialogue features sardonic wit, impatience with provincial stagnation,
  and fierce defense of the vulnerable.

=== 2. The Moral Sway Continuum Engine
* **Ethical Mechanics**: Discrete moral choices tracking across `Moral (Good)`
  and `Moral (Evil)` within the invariant continuum `[-100, +100]`.
* **The Test of Honesty (Eric's House, Map 28)**:
  - Stealing Eric's life savings (10G) rewards quick cash but plunges morality.
  - Donating 500G to cover Eric's rent saves a desperate worker and earns the *Ring of Fire*.
* **The Struggle of Dio Village (John the Thief vs. St. Baron, Map 30/31)**:
  - Feudal exploitation vs. outrider rebellion, forcing the heroes to choose
    between legal compliance and moral justice.

=== 3. Lecode Tactical Battle System (LeTBS) Mechanics
* **Grid Movement & Range Geometries**:
  - Movement Points (4 MP baseline), modified by skills like *Light Steps* (+1) and *Heavy Hit* (-3).
  - Scope shapes: `circle(r)`, `circle(max, min)` (annular), `line(n)`, `square(r)`.
* **Terrain Hazards & Obstacles**:
  - *Ice Wall* and *Ice Prison* creating physical destructible barriers (`HP: 20`).
  - *Volcano Ground* and *Burning Tile Spread* (`spread: 100% circle(1)`).
* **Turn Timeline Manipulation**:
  - *Accelerate*, *Decelerate*, and *Emergency Turn* altering the turn queue.

=== 4. The Monotonic 24-Hour Time Engine
* **Grandma's Watch**: Family heirloom activating real-time calendar and clock
  tracking (Seconds, Minutes, Hours, Days, Weeks, Months, Years), corresponding
  directly to Layer 0 `time_utils`.

= Volume IV: The 2017–2018 Demo Baseline & Historical Archive

Verified historical baseline extracted from the original RPG Maker MV datasets, detailing the complete scenario script, LeTBS mechanics, grimoire, and systems.

== Chapter 39: Historical Baseline Overview & Provenance

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/demo_baseline/README.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This documentation module establishes the comprehensive, verified baseline of the original **2017–2018 RPG Maker MV demo** for *Tenebris and Lux* ("Darkness and Light"). Extracted directly from the archival data directories (`Old Game Data` and `Old_Game_Data_2/data`), this repository anchors our modern C11 reimplementation to its authentic gameplay mechanics, narrative scripts, character arcs, and tactical systems.

== 1. Historical Provenance & Dataset Strata

Between 2014 and 2018, *Tenebris and Lux* was conceived as an ambitious Tactical Strategy RPG (TRPG) combining top-down exploration, puzzle mechanics, and grid-based turn strategy powered by Lecode's Tactical Battle System (LeTBS).

The historical game data is preserved across two distinct repository strata:

[cols="1,2,4", options="header"]
|===
| Dataset | Directory Anchor | Architectural Role & Characteristics
| **Folder 1: Primary Narrative & Morality Build** | `Old Game Data/` | Contains 37 maps, complete dual-protagonist dialogue branches, the morality engine (Compass of Morality, moral sway continuum, honesty tests), and the core Dio Village quest line (`versionId: 40730002`).
| **Folder 2: Advanced LeTBS Tactical Build** | `Old_Game_Data_2/data/` | Contains 43 maps, extended party members (Sebastien the Thief, Angela the Mage, Lucius the Priest), advanced LeTBS action parameters, full tactical state routines, and character generator assets (`versionId: 2078310`).
|===

== 2. Documentation Taxonomy

This baseline compendium is partitioned into specialized manuals covering every facet of the historical demo:

[cols="2,4", options="header"]
|===
| Document | Focus & Coverage
| **[scenario_and_walkthrough.adoc](scenario_and_walkthrough.adoc)** | Chronological narrative walkthrough, scene-by-scene script, branching dialogue for Elijah vs Katrina, Map 001 through Map 043, puzzle mechanics, and transfer topologies.
| **[cast_and_characters.adoc](cast_and_characters.adoc)** | Comprehensive character dossiers for protagonists (Elijah & Katrina), extended party members (Sebastien, Angela, Lucius), village elders (Grandma Magolia, Monica, Eric), court figures (King Jihad, Roté), and summons.
| **[combat_and_letbs_mechanics.adoc](combat_and_letbs_mechanics.adoc)** | Grid-based battle architecture, movement point costs, directional facing, line-of-sight scopes, AOE shapes (`circle`, `line`, `square`), LeTBS action sequences, and tactical states.
| **[classes_and_growth.adoc](classes_and_growth.adoc)** | 7 combat classes (Warrior/Knight, Mage, Priest, Summoner, Hero, Thief, Guard) plus Summons; complete stat growth matrices (Lv 1–99) and level-by-level skill learnings.
| **[grimoire_and_skills.adoc](grimoire_and_skills.adoc)** | Complete grimoire of all 75+ historical spells, martial techniques, movement abilities, MP/TP costs, damage formulas, and LeTBS sequence tags.
| **[bestiary_and_troops.adoc](bestiary_and_troops.adoc)** | Bestiary of all 22+ enemies and neutral entities (Dream Eaters, Magos, Bandits, Chests, Ice Blocks), stat parameters, AI behavior routines, and troop encounter formations.
| **[armory_and_inventory.adoc](armory_and_inventory.adoc)** | Weapons (Medusa's Sword), Armors (Heart Shield, Ring of Fire), Items (Potions, Magic Water), and Key Lore Relics (Grandma's Watch, Compass of Morality, Book of Monsters, Book of Items).
| **[systems_switches_variables.adoc](systems_switches_variables.adoc)** | Global game engines: Monotonic Time System / Day-Night Cycle, Morality System, and an exhaustive directory of all 100+ switches and variables.
| **[dataset_evolution_delta.adoc](dataset_evolution_delta.adoc)** | Side-by-side comparative delta analysis between `Old Game Data` and `Old_Game_Data_2/data`, detailing version discrepancies and modern C11 reconciliation strategies.
|===

== 3. Architectural Extraction Script

The entire documentation suite is fully reproducible from source data using our automated Python extraction pipeline:

[source,bash]
----
python3 docs/tools/extract_demo_data.py
----

This ensures absolute fidelity to the original 2017–2018 records as we rebuild the game in native C11.

This document preserves the complete chronological story script, scene transitions,
puzzle structures, and verbatim dialogues of the 2017–2018 demo. It documents both
the shared story progression and the distinct dual-protagonist narrative branches
between **Elijah Ro-enheart** (`\n[1]`) and **Lady Katrina Jaren** (`\n[2]`).

== Chapter 41: Complete Cast & Tactical Party Compendium

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/demo_baseline/cast_and_characters.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This compendium catalogues every character, party companion, village elder, court
antagonist, and mystical summon present in the 2017–2018 demo archives, detailing
their combat statistics, class associations, sprite bindings, and narrative significance.

== 1. Dual Protagonists

=== Elijah Ro-enheart (The Steadfast Vanguard)
* **ID**: `Actor 1`
* **Class**: Hero (`Class 5`) / Knight (`Class 1`)
* **Relic Affinity**: Bearer of the **Shield of Lux**
* **Initial Level**: Lv 5 (Folder 1) / Lv 1 (Folder 2)
* **Movement Points**: 4 MP
* **Sprite / Battler**: `Actor1_1` / `MainCharacter` (custom generator)
* **Profile & Personality**:
  Son of the vanished Commander Gareth Ro-enheart (former Head of the Royal Guard).
  Earnest, chivalric, deeply burdened by his father's mysterious disappearance on his
  10th birthday. Elijah seeks answers through service and duty, instinctively stepping
  forward to protect the weak and uphold the light of Sacyra.
* **Canonical LeTBS Specification**:
[source,text]
----
<letbs>
move_points: 4
auto_turn_order_face
auto_status_sprite
</letbs>
----

=== Lady Katrina Jaren (The Outrider Blade)
* **ID**: `Actor 2`
* **Class**: Hero (`Class 5`) / Start (`Class 7`)
* **Relic Affinity**: Wielder of the **Sword of Tenebris**
* **Initial Level**: Lv 5 (Folder 1) / Lv 1 (Folder 2)
* **Movement Points**: 4 MP
* **Initial Equipment**: Weapon 1 (Sword), Armor 2 (Hat), Armor 3 (Cloth)
* **Sprite / Battler**: `Actor1_8`
* **Profile & Personality**:
  Noble daughter of aristocratic House Jaren. Rebellious, razor-sharp, pragmatic, and
  fiercely independent. Refusing a cloistered aristocratic marriage or court seat, Katrina
  escaped to Dio Village with eyes on joining the independent Adventurers' Guild. Her
  caustic wit masks a deep empathy for ordinary folk crushed by royal neglect.
* **Canonical LeTBS Specification**:
[source,text]
----
<letbs>
move_points: 4
auto_turn_order_face
auto_status_sprite
</letbs>
----

== 2. Extended Tactical Party Companions (Folder 2 Additions)

In `Old_Game_Data_2`, three specialized tactical party members were integrated to expand the grid-based TRPG party:

=== Sebastien (The Infiltrator Outlaw)
* **ID**: `Actor 4`
* **Class**: Thief (`Class 6`)
* **Initial Level**: Lv 4
* **Sprite / Battler**: `Actor3_7`
* **Tactical AI & Role**: Outrider flanker equipped with `ranged_fighter` AI, trap disarmament, and precision critical strikes.

=== Angela (The Evoker Elementalist)
* **ID**: `Actor 5`
* **Class**: Mage (`Class 2`)
* **Initial Level**: Lv 4
* **Movement Scope**: `square(2)` (4 MP)
* **Sprite / Battler**: `Actor3_8` (custom cast, atk, hit, victory animations)
* **Action Palette**: Full tactical command set (`Move`, `Skill[42]`, `Items`, `Scan`, `Pass`).
* **Tactical Role**: Long-range elemental artillery, crowd control, and battlefield terrain modification (ice walls, volcano ground).

=== Lucius (The Battle Priest)
* **ID**: `Actor 6`
* **Class**: Priest (`Class 3`)
* **Initial Level**: Lv 4
* **Sprite / Battler**: `Actor2_7`
* **Tactical Role**: Turn order manipulation (`Accelerate`, `Decelerate`, `Emergency Turn`), long-distance allied teleportation, and barrier restoration.

== 3. Summoned Celestial Entities

* **Class**: `Class 12` | **Sprite**: `Nature` (Index 4)
* **Skills**: Air Compression (`Skill 132`), Great Wind (`Skill 133` - pushback), Transposition (`Skill 134` - swap places).

=== Pixie (Actor 12)
* **Class**: `Class 13` | **Sprite**: `Nature` (Index 5)

=== Fire Chicken (Actor 13)
* **Class**: `Class 14` | **Sprite**: `Nature` (Index 2)

== 4. Key Lore Figures & NPCs

[cols="1,2,4", options="header"]
|===
| Name | Role & Allegiance | Historical Significance
| **Grandma Magolia** | Matriarch of Dio Village | Mother of vanished Commander Gareth Ro-enheart; grandmother to Elijah. Bestows *Grandma's Watch* and *Compass of Morality*.
| **Monica the Mystic** | Former Royal Battlemage | Eccentric hermit and priestess of Goddess Lyri. Guides protagonists in awakening relic powers through ritual offerings.
| **Eric the Lumberjack** | Impoverished Dio Laborer | Loses his livelihood to monster raids; serves as the moral litmus test for player empathy in the *Test of Honesty*. Bestows the *Ring of Fire*.
| **John the Thief** | Local Outlaw Leader | Operates in Lower Dio; plots to unseat the predatory St. Baron to return wealth to the villagers.
| **The St. Baron** | Aristocratic Landowner | Feudal aristocrat bleeding Dio Village dry; symbol of court corruption and moral decay.
| **Chrono** | Archival Sage & Guardian | Guide of the Dream Chamber / Ancient Archives. Oversees archetype stone selection and mechanics trials.
| **High Priest Roté** | Royal Advisor | Court prelate who witnesses the cosmic omens and hastens to warn King Jihad.
| **King Jihad** | Monarch of Sacyra | Feigns royal mobilization while falling victim to the King's Curse, secretly coveting the returned primordial darkness to surpass The Dark Lord.
|===

== Chapter 42: LeTBS Tactical Grid System Specification

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/demo_baseline/combat_and_letbs_mechanics.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This document formalizes the grid-based Tactical Strategy RPG combat architecture
implemented via Lecode's Tactical Battle System (LeTBS) in the 2017–2018 demo.
It details movement costs, action economy, scope geometries, terrain manipulation,
and turn manipulation invariants.

== 1. Battle Field Architecture & Action Economy

* **Grid Structure**: 2D tile-based tactical grid mapped over encounter maps.
* **Movement Points (MP)**: Consumable per-turn budget determining movement range:
  - Baseline Protagonists & Allies: `move_points: 4`.
  - Heavy Impact Reductions: Heavy Hit reduces target MP by 3 (`move_points+: -3`).
  - Outrider Haste: Light Steps increases target MP by 1 (`move_points+: 1`).
* **Turn Timeline**: Monotonic timeline ordering turns by entity agility (`auto_turn_order_face`).
* **Directional Facing**: Facing impacts defense and positional abilities (e.g., *Phantom Slash* teleports directly behind the target before striking).

== 2. Scope & Area of Effect (AOE) Geometries

The demo configured rich geometric targeting parameters across skills:

[cols="2,2,3", options="header"]
|===
| Scope Pattern | Example Skill | Geometric Definition
| `circle(r)` | *Fire* (`Skill 42`), *Heal* (`Skill 72`) | Manhattan or Chebyshev circle of radius `r` centered on the caster.
| `circle(max_r, min_r)` | *Ghost Arrow* (`Skill 79`), *Dagger Throw* (`Skill 169`) | Annular targeting ring; cannot target adjacent cells within `min_r`.
| `line(n)` | *Explosive Mark* (`Skill 76`), *Great Wind* (`Skill 133`) | Cardinal linear projection up to `n` cells from caster.
| `square(r)` | *Angela Move Scope* (`Actor 5`) | Full square grid extent of radius `r`.
| `custom(pattern)` | *Fire Arrows* (`Skill 82`) | Scripted multi-target projectile pattern hitting all visible adversaries.
| `through_obstacles` | *Teleportation* (`Skill 73`) | Ignores intermediate terrain obstacles, requiring only a free destination cell.
|===

== 3. Terrain State Modifications & Dynamic Hazards

Combat encounters feature active environmental manipulation:

* **Volcano Ground (`Skill 47`)**: Lays hazardous fiery embers across multiple ground cells dealing continuous fire damage.
* **Ice Wall / Ice Prison (`Skill 51`, `Skill 52`)**: Summons physical destructible barricades (`HP: 20`) that obstruct enemy line-of-sight and movement corridors.
* **Explosive Mark & Magic Trap (`Skill 76`, `Skill 80`)**: Proximity-triggered grid landmines that detonate upon enemy entry.
* **Burning Tile Spread (`State 110`)**: Dynamic fire propagation expanding with `spread: 100% circle(1) all`.

== 4. Turn Timeline Manipulation

Unique priest techniques alter the flow of tactical battle:

* **Accelerate (`Skill 87`)**: Advances target position along the turn timeline by 100%.
* **Decelerate (`Skill 88`)**: Delays target position along the turn timeline by 100%.
* **Emergency Turn (`Skill 89`)**: Instantly grants an immediate action turn to an allied battler.
* **Extra Turn (`Skill 86`)**: Grants the caster or target an additional action cycle upon turn conclusion.

== Chapter 44: The Complete Grimoire (75+ Skills & Formulas)

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/demo_baseline/grimoire_and_skills.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This document records the complete grimoire of all 75+ active techniques, spells,
and summon abilities defined in `Skills.json`. It provides mathematical damage
formulas, MP/TP costs, scope parameters, and tactical LeTBS sequences.


== Basic Martial Maneuvers

[cols="^1,2,^1,^1,3,4", options="header"]
|===
| ID | Skill Name | MP | TP | Damage Formula | Description & Tactical Note
| `1` | **Attack** | 0 | 0 | `a.atk * 4 - b.def * 2` |  + 
_Note_: `Skill #1 will be used when you select the Attack command.`
| `2` | **Guard** | 0 | 0 | `0` |  + 
_Note_: `Skill #2 will be used when you select the Guard command.`
| `3` | **Dual Attack** | 0 | 0 | `a.atk * 4 - b.def * 2` | 
| `4` | **Double Attack** | 0 | 0 | `a.atk * 4 - b.def * 2` | 
| `5` | **Triple Attack** | 0 | 0 | `a.atk * 4 - b.def * 2` | 
| `6` | **Escape** | 0 | 0 | `0` | 
| `7` | **Wait** | 0 | 0 | `0` | 
|===


== Knight Martial Techniques

[cols="^1,2,^1,^1,3,4", options="header"]
|===
| ID | Skill Name | MP | TP | Damage Formula | Description & Tactical Note
| `11` | **Repulsive Blow** | 0 | 0 | `this.phyDmg(120)` | Hits and push the target for two cell. + 
_Note_: `<letbs> scope: circle(1) sequence: atk_push(2) scope_color: #FFF </letbs>  <l...`
| `12` | **Flash Jump** | 0 | 0 | `this.phyDmg(110) + this.magDmg(10)` | Jumps to an available position in the scope and invoke  a thunder to damage nearby fighters. + 
_Note_: `<letbs> scope: circle(3,1) aoe: circle(1) scope_options: need_free_cell aoe_o...`
| `13` | **Rush** | 0 | 0 | `this.phyDmg(80)` | Rushes toward the target. + 
_Note_: `<letbs> scope: custom(rush) sequence: rush </letbs>  <letbs_AI> type: offense...`
| `14` | **Intimidating Aura** | 0 | 0 | `(a.atk * 4 - b.def * 2) * 1.2` | Casts an aura that reduce enemies' ATK by 15%. Grants as well 70% counter attack chance. +

== 2. Neutral & Interactive Grid Entities

The demo utilized the battle engine to render interactive tactical grid entities:
* **Wooden Chest (`Enemy 35`)**: `HP: 50` | Yields 200 Gold upon destruction via Common Event 5.
* **Silver Chest (`Enemy 36`)**: `HP: 50` | Yields 2,000 Gold upon destruction via Common Event 6.
* **Golden Chest (`Enemy 37`)**: `HP: 50` | Yields 10,000 Gold upon destruction via Common Event 7.
* **Ice Block (`Enemy 34`)**: `HP: 20` | Destructible terrain obstacle summoned by Ice Wall.
* **Stolen Goods (`Enemy 38`)**: `HP: 1` | Objective dropped upon defeat of Mage Bandit.
* **Invincible Neutral (`Enemy 32`)**: `HP: 99,999` | Static grid anchor.

== Chapter 47: Engine Systems, Time Clock & Switch/Variable Index

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/game_design/demo_baseline/systems_switches_variables.adoc`

[abstract]
== Overview

This document specifies the global state machines, monotonic time clock, morality engine,
and the complete registry of all switches and variables governing demo logic.

== 1. The Monotonic Time Engine

The time system is driven by Common Events 1 and 2, updating a 24-hour cycle:
* `Variable 1`: Seconds (0–59)
* `Variable 2`: Minutes (0–59)
* `Variable 3`: Hours (1–12)
* `Variable 4`: 24-Hour Military Format (0–23)
* `Variable 5`: Days (1–7: Sunday through Saturday)
* `Variable 6`: Weeks
* `Variable 7`: Months
* `Variable 8`: Years

In the modern C11 architecture, this corresponds to Layer 0 `time_utils` (`time_get_monotonic_ms`).

== 2. The Moral Sway Continuum Engine

The morality engine tracks the party's ethical orientation on an invariant scale:
* `Variable 9`: **Moral (Overall)**: Continuum within `[-100, +100]`.
* `Variable 10`: **Moral (Good)**: Cumulative points awarded for altruism, charity, and mercy.
* `Variable 11`: **Moral (Evil)**: Cumulative points accrued for ruthless, self-serving, or thieving acts.
* `Item 8`: **Compass of Morality**: Tool allowing the player to inspect their moral continuum in the field.

| `15` | **Deposit Amount** | Global numerical state accumulator
|===

== Overview

This document provides a comparative delta between `Old Game Data` (Folder 1) and
`Old_Game_Data_2/data` (Folder 2), detailing the evolution from narrative prototype
to advanced LeTBS tactical simulation, and establishing modern C11 reconciliation strategies.

== 1. Dataset Characteristics & Metrics

| Subsystem Metric | Folder 1 (Old Game Data) | Folder 2 (Old_Game_Data_2/data)

== 2. Core Differences & Evolution Strategy

1. **Narrative vs. Tactical Pivot**:
   - **Folder 1** represents the *narrative-complete* edition of the demo. It contains all dialogue branches, the Dio Village moral tests (Eric's Honesty Pot, John the Thief vs St. Baron), and key relics like the *Compass of Morality*.
   - **Folder 2** represents the *tactical engine expansion*. It introduces full party combat with Angela, Sebastien, and Lucius, 6 extra dungeon maps, and rich LeTBS states (e.g. *Burning Tile Spread*, *Charging*, *Intimidating Aura*).

2. **C11 Reconstruction Recommendation**:
   - **Layer 1 Simulation Engine**: Combine the narrative quest topology and moral continuum from Folder 1 with the rich 5-character tactical class abilities and LeTBS movement/scope geometry from Folder 2.
   - **Layer 2 Presentation**: Maintain the authentic dialog scripts, dual-protagonist branch voices, and environmental puzzles.
