== Chapter 60: DevLog 005: Ontological Sub-Assemblies & 80-Column Discipline

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/10/architecture/devlog_005_ontological_code_layering_and_docstrings.adoc`

[abstract]
== Abstract & Engineering Intent

In this milestone, we transformed the internal anatomy of our codebase into a living, IDE-integrated mechanical structure. Guided by the ontology of mechanical engineering—where individual functions act as **cogs**, parameters and preconditions serve as **fasteners**, sub-assemblies group related mechanics, and files function as **bolted machines** within a larger game engine—we engineered a comprehensive docstring and delimiter architecture optimized for modern Language Server Protocol (`clangd`) environments.

Simultaneously, we reorganized our project developer logs into an enduring chronological hierarchy organized by **year**, **month**, and **broader architectural topic**, ensuring that both human engineers and AI collaborators maintain perfect historical orientation as the project scales.

---

== 1. The Need for IDE-First Hover Documentation

Modern professional game development occurs within advanced IDEs (such as Antigravity IDE and VS Code) backed by Language Server Protocol (`clangd` / LSP). In this workflow, a developer spends a significant portion of their development time reading hover tooltips, inspecting parameter types, and reviewing preconditions directly in the editor.

Prior to this refactor, hovering over a function such as `math_clamp_int` revealed basic type signatures or truncated comments. Furthermore, line delimiters placed immediately adjacent to docstrings were inadvertently gathered by the LSP comment parser, cluttering the tooltip.

### 1.1 The Clangd Hover Contract

To produce clean, beautiful, and informative hover windows in the IDE:
1. **Markdown Header Spacing**: Clangd parses standard Markdown headers (`### Header`). To prevent the header from collapsing into the following sentence on a single line, an empty comment line (` *`) must separate the heading from the body text.
2. **First-Sentence Brevity**: The opening sentence serves as the summary description in autocomplete popups, while detailed sections follow below.
3. **Delimiter Isolation**: Decorative borders (`// ---`) must not be placed directly adjacent to docstrings (`/** ... */`) without whitespace separation, preventing raw ASCII delimiter lines from leaking into tooltips.
4. **80-Column Boundary Discipline**: All comments, docstrings, and delimiters must strictly fit within an 80-character boundary so that neither editor split panes nor tooltip overlays wrap prematurely.

---

== 2. The Machine Metaphor: From Cosmos to Fasteners

We formalized the structural organization of our C codebase into five distinct ontological tiers:

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        COSMOS: TENEBRIS AND LUX                        │
│                 (The Complete Tactical Strategy RPG)                   │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     STRATUM: ARCHITECTURAL LAYER                       │
│    Layer 0: Core Utilities | Layer 1: Simulation Engine | Layer 2: GUI │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                       MACHINE: SUBSYSTEM MODULE                        │
│    e.g. core.util.math | core.util.geom | core.util.string | time      │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                  ASSEMBLY: SUB-CATEGORY / FUNCTION GROUP               │
│    e.g. [ SUB-ASSEMBLY 3.1: INTEGER CLAMPING & SATURATION COGS ]      │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                        COG: INDIVIDUAL FUNCTION                        │
│    e.g. math_clamp_int() | geom_rect_intersects() | str_safe_copy()   │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                   FASTENERS: NUTS, BOLTS & TRIPWIRES                   │
│    Invariants, Preconditions, Clamping Floors, Return Error Codes      │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

1. **Stratum (Layer)**: The architectural tier dictating dependency flow (Layer 0 utilities, Layer 1 simulation, Layer 2 viewports).
2. **Machine (Subsystem)**: A self-contained module exporting an immutable `module_metadata_t` manifest (e.g. `core.util.math`).
3. **Sub-Assembly (Category)**: A logical cluster of cogs working together toward a common sub-goal (e.g. *Integer Clamping & Saturation*, *Equilibrium Steering*, *AABB Hit-Testing*).
4. **Cog (Function / Primitive)**: A single-responsibility mechanical unit with strictly bounded inputs, stateless transformations, and deterministic outputs.
5. **Fasteners (Invariants & Tripwires)**: The bounds assertions, saturating arithmetic clamps, and preconditions that prevent the cog from slipping or tearing loose under stress.

---

== 3. Visual Border & Delimiter Standards

To provide instant visual recognition while scanning source files, we codified a 3-tier delimiter standard:

* **Level 1: Block Lifecycle Banner** (80 columns, double equal border `==`):
+
[source,c]
----
// =============================================================================
// BLOCK 3: BODY BLOCK
// Stratum: Layer 0 (Core Utilities) | Machine: Bounded Arithmetic Engine
// =============================================================================
----
* **Level 2: Sub-Assembly Banner** (80 columns, single dash border `--`):
+
[source,c]
----
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// [ SUB-ASSEMBLY 3.1: INTEGER CLAMPING & SATURATION COGS ]
// Mission: Guardrail calculations against integer overflow and underflow.
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
----
* **Level 3: Isolated Cog Docstring**:
+
[source,c]
----
/**
 * Clamps a signed integer to a closed interval [min_val, max_val].
 *
 * ### Machine Role & Mission
 *
 * Serves as a foundational mathematical guardrail preventing overflow,
 * underflow, or boundary corruption across player statistics, resonance
 * meters, and moral sway values. If min_val > max_val, boundaries swap.
 *
 * ### Ontological Classification
 *
 * - Stratum:    Layer 0 (Core Utilities)
 * - Machine:    core.util.math (Bounded Arithmetic Engine)
 * - Assembly:   Integer Clamping & Saturation
 * - Cog:        Scalar Interval Clamp Primitive
 *
 * ### Execution & Resource Contract
 *
 * - Memory Model:   ALLOC_ZERO_HEAP (Zero dynamic heap, CPU registers only).
 * - Concurrency:    REENTRANT_PURE (Stateless, deterministic, thread-safe).
 * - Latency Budget: < 1 microsecond execution time (O(1) branching).
 *
 * @param[in] val     The raw input integer to evaluate.
 * @param[in] min_val Lower boundary limit (inclusive).
 * @param[in] max_val Upper boundary limit (inclusive).
 *
 * @return Clamped value satisfying `min_val <= result <= max_val`.
 *
 * @pre  `min_val <= max_val` under normal operation.
 * @post Result resides strictly within `[min_val, max_val]`.
 *
 * @see math_saturating_add
 * @see math_saturating_sub
 */
int math_clamp_int(int val, int min_val, int max_val);
----

---

```text
        └── lore_and_design/
            └── devlog_002_the_sacyra_chronicles_and_trpg_vision.adoc
```

* **Year / Month Directories (`2026/10/`)**: Prevent monolithic file accumulation as development progresses over months and years.
* **Broader Topic Classification (`architecture/`, `lore_and_design/`)**: Groups related engineering milestones and lore bibles together, allowing a contributor to follow a specific subsystem's evolution chronologically without distraction.

== Chapter 61: DevLog 006: Source Implementation & Root Pedagogical Commenting

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/10/architecture/devlog_006_source_and_root_pedagogical_commenting.adoc`

[abstract]
== Executive Summary

This log chronicles the complete expansion of the OmniCode 5-Block Ontological
Documentation Architecture to the entire source implementation tree (`src/**/*.c`)
and root configuration anchors (`CMakeLists.txt`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`).
Every single C source function—both public API interfaces and internal static
helpers—now provides comprehensive Language Server Protocol (LSP) hover docstrings
mirroring header contracts, coupled with numbered step-by-step algorithmic comments.
Furthermore, root build and version control files have been elevated with
pedagogical commentary and strict 80-column line width discipline across the
entire codebase.

== 1. Architectural Intent: End-to-End Documentation Consistency

While header files (`include/**/*.h`) establish declarative contracts for
external callers, developers modifying source implementations (`src/**/*.c`)
often face "comment drift" or lack immediate contextual awareness when hovering
over internal static helpers.

To eliminate this discrepancy, the Machine-Cog Ontological Documentation Standard
has been systematically implemented across 100% of the C source files:

1. **Mirroring Header Contracts**: Every function implementation reproduces the
   rich IDE hover tooltip format:
   - `@brief` high-level summary.
   - `### Machine Role & Mission` explaining tactical/engine purpose.
   - `### Ontological Classification` (Stratum -> Machine -> Assembly -> Cog).
   - `### Execution & Resource Contract` (Memory Model, Concurrency, Latency).
   - Doxygen parameter (`@param`) and return (`@return`) declarations.
2. **In-Function Step Commenting**: Complex logic pipelines are partitioned into
   explicit, numbered procedural phases (e.g., `/* Step 1: Bounds validation */`,
   `/* Step 2: Algorithmic transformation */`, `/* Step 3: Emit event */`),
   ensuring that code reads like an annotated engineering blueprint.

== 3. Root Configuration Pedagogical Enhancement

Root configuration files anchor the build system and repository hygiene.
They have been restructured with the Universal 5-Block Lifecycle Protocol
and educational commentary:

=== 3.1 `CMakeLists.txt`
- **Block 1 (Metadata)**: Project provenance, minimum CMake version (3.24),
  and ISO C11 standard invariants.
- **Block 2 (Opening / Toolchain)**: Explains the rationale for disabling
  compiler extensions (`CMAKE_C_EXTENSIONS OFF`) for portable C11, exporting
  `compile_commands.json` for LSP tooling (clangd), locating native X11 windowing,
  and configuring warning flags (`-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` / `/W4`).
- **Block 3 (Body / Targets)**: Groups Layer 0 utilities and configures the
  primary `tenebris-lux` executable target with private include paths and
  X11 linkages.
- **Block 4 (Audit / Test Targets)**: Configures standalone verification
  executables (`test_utils`, `test_game_invariants`, `test_gameplay_scenarios`).
- **Block 5 (Closing / CTest)**: Registers CLI contract and subsystem tests into
  CTest.

=== 3.2 `.gitignore`
- **Block 1 (Metadata)**: Version control exclusion contracts and repository
  hygiene invariants.
- **Block 2 (Compiled Binaries)**: Groups object code (`*.o`, `*.obj`),
  static libraries (`*.a`, `*.lib`), dynamic libraries (`*.so`, `*.dylib`),
  and native executables.
- **Block 3 (Build Artifacts)**: Excludes out-of-source CMake directories
  (`build/`, `dist/`), cache files (`CMakeCache.txt`), and temporary makefiles.
- **Block 4 (Telemetry & Logs)**: Excludes transient runtime traces (`*.log`,
  `*.audit`) and experimental scratch directories.
- **Block 5 (IDE & OS Detritus)**: Excludes `.vscode/`, `.idea/`, `.cache/`,
  macOS `.DS_Store`, and editor swap files.

=== 3.3 `README.md`
- Synchronized documentation indices to point to the chronological calendar
  archive in `docs/dev_logs/2026/10/README.adoc`.

"

== Chapter 62: DevLog 007: Archival Extraction & The 2017–2018 Demo Baseline

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/10/lore_and_design/devlog_007_rpg_maker_demo_data_extraction_and_baseline.adoc`

[abstract]
== Abstract

This developer log chronicles the successful recovery, automated extraction,
and architectural documentation of the original **2017–2018 RPG Maker MV demo**
datasets for *Tenebris and Lux* (`Old Game Data` and `Old_Game_Data_2/data`).
It establishes a permanent, verifiable baseline of the high school genesis era,
cataloguing the complete 43-map scenario script, the dual-protagonist narrative
branches (Elijah Ro-enheart & Lady Katrina Jaren), the grid-based Lecode
Tactical Battle System (LeTBS), the 75-skill grimoire, and the morality engine.
This baseline serves as our living blueprint as we systematically reconstruct
the game in native C11.

== 1. Historical Context: Rediscovery of the Genesis Datasets

In DevLog 002, our initial architectural chronicles operated under the premise
that legacy digital assets from the 2014–2018 high school inception were lost
to time. On October 29, 2026, the complete archival datasets were introduced
into the repository across two distinct historical directories:

1. **`Old Game Data` (Folder 1: Primary Narrative & Morality Build)**:
   - Version ID: `40730002` | Game Title: *"Tenebris and Lux"*
   - 36 map files + complete database (Actors, Classes, Skills, Items, Weapons,
     Armors, Enemies, Troops, States, CommonEvents, MapInfos).
   - Preserves complete dual-protagonist dialogue branches, the *Compass of
     Morality*, the *Test of Honesty* with Eric the Lumberjack, John the Thief's
     rebellion against the St. Baron, and the monotonic 24-hour clock.

2. **`Old_Game_Data_2/data` (Folder 2: Advanced LeTBS Tactical Build)**:
   - Version ID: `2078310` | Game Title: *"Tenebris & Lux"*
   - 43 map files + extended tactical party companions (Sebastien the Thief,
     Angela the Mage, Lucius the Priest).
   - Preserves advanced LeTBS tactical combat metadata (movement point costs,
     scope geometry, AOE patterns, turn timeline manipulation, and character
     generator presets `Grandma.json` and `TheKing.json`).

== 2. The Extraction Toolchain: `docs/tools/extract_demo_data.py`

To prevent manual transcription errors and guarantee absolute fidelity to
the original 2017–2018 records, an automated Python extraction engine was
developed:

[source,bash]
----
python3 docs/tools/extract_demo_data.py
----

=== Core Functions of the Extraction Engine
* **Cross-Dataset Reconciliation**: Ingests both `Old Game Data` and
  `Old_Game_Data_2/data`, cross-referencing actors, classes, skills, and map
  events to capture both the rich narrative branches of Folder 1 and the
  expanded tactical combat systems of Folder 2.
* **Verbatim Script Mining**: Parses RPG Maker MV event command codes
  (`101` text headers, `401` text lines, `102` choices, `121` switches,
  `122` variables, `201` transfers, `301` battles) to reconstruct the exact
  dialogue transcripts for both Elijah (`\n[1]`) and Katrina (`\n[2]`).
* **Tactical Metadata Extraction**: Extracts LeTBS XML/YAML-style note tags
  from skills, states, and actors, formalizing movement points, range shapes,
  cooldowns, and AI behavior patterns (`ranged_fighter`, `ai_no_attack`).

== 3. The 10-Volume Baseline Documentation Compendium

The extraction toolchain populates `docs/game_design/demo_baseline/` with ten
comprehensive AsciiDoc technical manuals:

[cols="1,2,3", options="header"]
|===
| Document | Primary File | Core Contents
| **Master Index** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/README.adoc[README.adoc] | Provenance, dataset taxonomy, and reconstruction methodology.
| **Scenario Script** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/scenario_and_walkthrough.adoc[scenario_and_walkthrough.adoc] | 7-Act narrative script across 43 maps, branching dialogues, and puzzle solutions.
| **Dramatis Personae** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/cast_and_characters.adoc[cast_and_characters.adoc] | Protagonists (Elijah, Katrina), allies (Sebastien, Angela, Lucius), and key NPCs.
| **Tactical Mechanics** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/combat_and_letbs_mechanics.adoc[combat_and_letbs_mechanics.adoc] | LeTBS 2D grid rules, MP economy, scope geometry, and terrain hazards.
| **Class Progressions** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/classes_and_growth.adoc[classes_and_growth.adoc] | 7 classes + summons; Lv 1–99 stat matrices; level-by-level skill learnings.
| **Grimoire** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/grimoire_and_skills.adoc[grimoire_and_skills.adoc] | 75+ active techniques, damage formulas, and LeTBS sequence tags.
| **Bestiary & Troops** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/bestiary_and_troops.adoc[bestiary_and_troops.adoc] | 22+ enemies, destructible chests/ice blocks, AI tags, and troop formations.
| **Armory & Items** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/armory_and_inventory.adoc[armory_and_inventory.adoc] | Weapons, armors, consumables, and quest relics (Grandma's Watch, Compass).
| **Systems & Switches** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/systems_switches_variables.adoc[systems_switches_variables.adoc] | Monotonic clock, moral continuum, and full directory of 100+ switches/variables.
| **Evolution Delta** | xref:../../game_design/demo_baseline/dataset_evolution_delta.adoc[dataset_evolution_delta.adoc] | Technical comparison between Folder 1 and Folder 2 with C11 roadmap.
|===

== 4. C11 Engine Rebuilding Roadmap

With the historical baseline fully documented, the path forward for our native
C11 implementation is clear, disciplined, and rigorous:

1. **Layer 0 Core Expansion (`include/util/`, `src/util/`)**:
   - Align `time_utils` to faithfully model the 24-hour calendar cycle driven by
     *Grandma's Watch*.
   - Expand `geom_utils` to support discrete grid-cell raycasting and LeTBS
     range shapes (`circle`, `line`, `square`, `through_obstacles`).
2. **Layer 1 Simulation Expansion (`include/sim/`, `src/sim/`)**:
   - Reconstruct the tactical battle state machine supporting 5-member party
     formations and enemy AI routines.
   - Implement the moral sway continuum engine (`[-100, +100]`) tracked by the
     *Compass of Morality*.
3. **Layer 2 Viewport & Presentation (`include/gui/`, `src/gui/`)**:
   - Render the 2D tactical grid with movement overlays, Zone of Control, and
     turn order timelines.
   - Implement the dialogue presentation window capable of rendering branching
     scripts with character portraits.
4. **Layer 4 Automated Verification (`tests/`)**:
   - Author invariant test suites verifying deterministic grid movement,
     damage formula parity, and softlock immunity under all quest choice trees.

== Chapter 63: DevLog 008: Canonical Skills, The Dreamscape & Ground-Truth Pacing

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/10/lore_and_design/devlog_008_canonical_skills_and_dreamscape_baseline.adoc`

[abstract]
== Abstract

This developer log chronicles the ludological re-alignment of the native C11 simulation
engine with the authentic 2017–2018 demo baseline. It details the transition from generic
combat verbs to concrete tactical skills: Defend (*Lux Barrio* with turn duration rolls
and $25\%$ damage mitigation), Attack (*Cuttatio Tenebris* with $20\%$ area splash cleave),
and Special (*Basic Magic*, *Basic Ward*, *Basic Charm*, and *Duo-Loca Bashin n Slash* with
adaptive defense targeting). Furthermore, it documents the critical correction of the
antagonist identity to **"The Dark Lord"** (the overarching background "A Plot"), the
reinstatement of **The Dreamscape** opening trial, and protagonist customization.

== 1. Lessons Learned: Game Developer vs. Software CRUD Thinking

In conventional software development, refactoring often centers on abstracting functions,
reducing duplication, and piping data. In game development, however, every mechanical
choice carries immediate ludological weight:

1. **Mechanical Texture Over Generic Verbs**:
   - Generic commands like "ward" and "strike" felt mechanically hollow without distinct
     tactical identities.
   - Transforming "Ward" into **`Lux Barrio`** introduces active damage mitigation ($-25\%$)
     and tactical duration rolls ($2 \dots 5$ turns). Defending ceases to be a passive stall
     and becomes a strategic barrier phase.
   - Transforming "Strike" into **`Cuttatio Tenebris`** with $20\%$ flank splash damage
     solves the issue of target tunneling, rewarding spatial positioning against swarms.

2. **Ground Truth Over Synthetic Assumptions**:
   - In earlier drafts, the antagonist was arbitrarily labeled "Dark Lord Malakor".
     Forensic inspection of the original 2017–2018 demo revealed that in canon he was
     consistently referred to as **"The Dark Lord"**.
   - The Dark Lord is an omnipresent cosmic presence ("The A Plot") menacing from afar,
     while immediate gameplay focuses on regional factions, village survival, and
     ancient trials. Rushing into the final boss ruins narrative pacing.

3. **Narrative Pacing & The Dreamscape**:
   - The game does not begin in a high-stakes fortress war; it begins in **The Dreamscape**
     (guided by Chrono facing Dream Eaters), allowing the player to awaken spiritually
     before physically waking up in Dio Village at Grandma Magolia's hearth.

== 2. Implementation Telemetry & Invariant Verification

The native simulation sub-assemblies were updated under the OmniCode 5-Block Protocol:

* **Layer 1 Simulation**:
  - `src/sim/sim_types.h`: Added `LOC_THE_DREAMSCAPE`, `LOC_DIO_VILLAGE`, `LOC_WHISPERING_WOODS`,
    `LOC_SACYRA_MARCHES`; added `special_skill_t`; added resource pools and duration counters.
  - `src/sim/combat.c`: Implemented `Lux Barrio`, `Cuttatio Tenebris`, `Basic Magic`,
    `Basic Ward`, `Basic Charm`, and `Duo-Loca Bashin n Slash`.
  - `src/sim/dispatcher.c`: Tokenizes new commands, handles turn progression, decrements
    buff durations, and supports protagonist customization (`name <str>`).
* **Layer 4 Verification**:
  - `tests/test_game_invariants.c`: 7/7 suites passing.
  - `tests/test_gameplay_scenarios.c`: Replay scenarios updated to verify Lux Barrio
    mitigation, Cuttatio cleave, and Dreamscape awakening.
  - `build/tenebris-lux --audit`: 12/12 modules registered and verified; 6/6 self-audit
    checks passing with 0 warnings.

== Chapter 64: DevLog 009: Macro-History, Thematic Triad & Multi-Ending Architecture

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/11/lore_and_design/devlog_009_macro_history_thematic_triad_and_multi_ending_architecture.adoc`

[abstract]
== Abstract

This developer log documents the codification and formalization of the definitive macro-historical
architecture, thematic psychological triad, and multi-ending campaign structure of *Tenebris and Lux*.
Synthesizing the recovered 2017–2018 RPG Maker MV demo baseline (Folders 1 & 2) with the native ISO C11
simulation engine, this milestone formalizes the 3,000-year dynastic wave from the First King to King Jihad,
the psychological triad linking past (Usuru, Casatti, Mi-ren) to present (Elijah, Katrina, Custom Bearer),
the etiology of "The King's Curse," the evolution of Grandma's Watch into The Dream Compass, and the
five-route ending matrix.

== 1. Macro-Historical Canon: The 3,000-Year Dynastic Wave

Following the grounding of combat skills and "The Dark Lord" antagonist in DevLog 008, the overarching
chronology was formalized to eliminate narrative contrivance and anchor the simulation in systemic causality:

1. **The Inception of Sacyra (Era I–III):**
   - The Shield of Lux and Sword of Tenebris were originally forged as ordinary, non-magical symbols of
     Peace and War under the First King's golden reign.
   - Following the inaugural week-long Festival of Peoples, the First King collapsed into a coma, his spirit
     lucid across the gossamer veil in The Dreamscape.
   - His physical death during the 5th Festival inaugurated a protective vigil by teenage sons Mi-ren and
     Casatti, sustaining ethereal projections during festival week until Mi-ren officially came "of age."
2. **The 11th Year Rupture & The 7-Year Great War (Era IV–V):**
   - An eleven-year-old wanderer from remote, communication-starved Libira accidentally tampered with the
     projection ritual, permanently sealing the First King in The Dreamscape.
   - The shock birthed raw **Tenebris** into reality. Taking the name **Usuru**, the youth led a six-power
     coalition war against the Capital.
   - Prince Casatti infused Tenebris into the Sword of War for **Absolute Justice**; Prince Mi-ren discovered
     **Lux** to defend the realm and seek Usuru's forgiveness.
   - Demonstrating that Lux tempers Tenebris but dissipates under violent emotional shifts, the war concluded
     in seven years under the federated **"Joint Realm, Separate Kingdoms"** accord and the **Prophecy of Kings**.
3. **The 3,000-Year Dynastic Wave:**
   - Spanning 30 to 50 monarchs across three millennia, the succession oscillates between Long Stasis Crests
     (60–90+ years), Tether-Sickness Troughs (5–20 years), and Bureaucratic Consolidations (35–50 years).
   - This established order exposes frontier neglect (Dio Village) as systemic apathy and transforms
     King Jihad from a petty villain into an existential traitor.

== 2. The Thematic Triad: "Trauma is Inherited, Destiny is Chosen"

Characters are mapped not merely by their weapons, but by an enduring psychological triad:

* **The Frontier Wound:** **Usuru** (Past) mirrors **Elijah Ro-enheart** (Present). Both endure ten years
  of childhood reverence before experiencing a fracture in their tenth/eleventh year. While Usuru succumbed
  to wrath, Elijah raises the Shield of Lux with independent conscience, achieving redemptive grace.
* **The Renouncing Blade:** **Prince Casatti** (Past) mirrors **Lady Katrina Jaren** (Present). Both renounce
  high aristocratic privilege (the High Throne / House Jaren) in pursuit of empirical truth and meritocratic
  brotherhood (The Academy / The Adventurers' Guild), wielding the Sword of Tenebris to slice through hypocrisy.
* **The Steward of Balance:** **Prince Mi-ren** (Past) mirrors **The Custom Bearer (`<chosen_name>`)** (Present).
  Both bear the burden of holding a fractured world together across the veil, synthesizing both perspectives
  to achieve true Equilibrium.

== 3. Ludological Architecture & Multi-Ending Specification

1. **The King's Curse as a Planar Wound:**
   - Court madness is revealed not as a demonic hex, but as the open planar wound beneath the High Throne
     left when Usuru shattered the stasis ritual. King Jihad's megalomania inverted the conduit, leading to
     possession by the Dark Lord's psychic remnant.
2. **The Midpoint Fork & Player Consent:**
   - Defeating King Jihad triggers the campaign fork. Players skipping missable nocturnal Dreamscape quests
     reach the **Neutral Ending** (saving Sacyra while leaving the First King marooned and the cycle dormant).
   - Players completing nocturnal Dream Compass trials unlock **The True Ending ("The Ugly Truth")**, which
     dismantles the comfortable propaganda of both factions and reconciles the realm.
3. **The Five Ending Routes:**
   - *Route A:* The Canonical Harmony (True Equilibrium Ending — Elijah + Katrina tandem).
   - *Route B:* The Benevolent Stasis (Neutral-Good / The Mi-ren Fallacy — `+100` Moral Sway).
   - *Route C:* The Calcified Tyrant (Neutral-Bad / Inquisitorial Stasis — `-100` Moral Sway).
   - *Route D:* The Abyssal Usurper (Neutral-Bad / The Cycle of Usuru — `-100` Moral Sway).
   - *Route E:* The Secret Chronicler Ending (Custom Bearer 100% Route).

== 4. Verification Telemetry

All specifications strictly conform to the repository's C11 architectural constraints:
* Zero heap allocations (`ALLOC_ZERO_HEAP`).
* Layer 0 monotonic clock tracking seconds, minutes, hours, weekdays, weeks, and seasons.
* Layer 1 bounded simulation invariants: `player_state_t` moral sway `[-100, +100]`, shield power `[0, 100]`,
  and edge sharpness `[0, 100]`.
* 12/12 pre-flight modules verified; zero compilation warnings under `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic`.

== Chapter 65: DevLog 010: Layer 0 Subsystem Modularization & Data-Driven Architecture

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/11/architecture/devlog_010_layer_0_subsystem_modularization_and_data_driven_architecture.adoc`

[abstract]
== Executive Summary

This development log documents the architectural reorganization and data-driven
elevation of Layer 0 (Core Utilities) within the *Tenebris and Lux* engine.
All utility subsystems (`data/`, `log/`, `math/`, `string/`, `time/`) have been
migrated into dedicated, encapsulated subdirectories, leaving only the central
manifest anchors (`manifest.h` and `manifest.c`) at the root of `include/util/`
and `src/util/`. Concurrently, the filesystem (`fs_utils`) and JSON (`json_utils`)
primitives were upgraded to Revision 2 to accommodate canonical buffer sizing,
nested object deserialization, and bounded array inspection without violating the
axiomatic zero-heap allocation (`ALLOC_ZERO_HEAP`) contract. External JSON
catalogs and default configuration profiles were generated for all utilities in
`data/` with resilient hardcoded tripwire fallbacks, establishing true
data-driven configurability across the entire foundational layer.

[quote, ProfessorSeanEX, CreativeWorkzStudio Architecture Council]
____
When every subsystem is housed in its own ontological domain, clarity of intent
replaces cognitive drift. Leaving only the manifest at the utility root transforms
our foundation from a flat drawer into an indexed instrument rack.
____

== 1. Architectural Motivation & The Problem of Flat Foundations

Prior to this milestone, Layer 0 suffered from structural asymmetry. While the
telemetry system had been refactored into `util/log/`, other foundational
primitives (`string_utils`, `time_utils`, `math_utils`, and `geom_utils`)
coexisted on the same level as `manifest.h` and `manifest.c`.

Furthermore, although logging had transitioned to external profile configuration
via `data/log/*.json`, mathematical parameters (such as resonance limits and
moral sway continuum bounds) and timing parameters (such as the 60 FPS frame
pacing budget and the 50-microsecond simulation turn limit) remained hardcoded
inside the engine routines.

=== 1.1 Structural Hygiene Objectives

1. **Root Directory Cleanliness**: Maintain only `manifest.h` and `manifest.c` at
   the root of `include/util/` and `src/util/`.
2. **Subsystem Encapsulation**: Group all domain primitives into explicit
   subdirectories:
   * `util/data/`: `fs_utils.h`, `json_utils.h`, `fs_utils.c`, `json_utils.c`
   * `util/log/`: `log.h`, `log_fallback.h`, `log.c`, `log_fallback.c`
   * `util/math/`: `math_utils.h`, `geom_utils.h`, `math_utils.c`, `geom_utils.c`
   * `util/string/`: `string_utils.h`, `string_utils.c`
   * `util/time/`: `time_utils.h`, `time_utils.c`
3. **Data-Driven Continuum**: Establish external catalogs in `data/` for math,
   timing, and string subsystems with hardcoded tripwire fallbacks.
4. **Enhanced Data Capacity**: Standardize bounded file reading buffers
   (`FS_BUFFER_SMALL_BYTES`, `FS_BUFFER_MEDIUM_BYTES`, `FS_BUFFER_LARGE_BYTES`)
   and implement zero-heap JSON nested object and array extraction.

== 2. Subsystem Stratification & Pathing Standards

Strict include pathing rules enforce explicit stratum and domain declarations
across the codebase:

[source,c]
----
#include "util/manifest.h"
#include "util/data/fs_utils.h"
#include "util/data/json_utils.h"
#include "util/log/log.h"
#include "util/math/math_utils.h"
#include "util/math/geom_utils.h"
#include "util/string/string_utils.h"
#include "util/time/time_utils.h"
----

Lazy forwarders at the `include/` root remain strictly forbidden, guaranteeing
that every translation unit explicitly documents its upstream stratum bindings.

== 3. Revision 2 Filesystem & JSON Engines

=== 3.1 Zero-Heap Filesystem Engine (`core.util.fs` Rev 2)

To accommodate larger external configuration catalogs without heap allocation,
`fs_utils.h` establishes canonical buffer sizing constants:

[cols="1,1,3", options="header"]
|===
| Buffer Constant | Capacity | Intended Machine Role
| `FS_BUFFER_SMALL_BYTES` | 1,024 B | Headers, status lines, and short tokens
| `FS_BUFFER_MEDIUM_BYTES` | 8,192 B | Standard profile configurations (< 8 KB)
| `FS_BUFFER_LARGE_BYTES` | 32,768 B | Aggregate catalogs and world node graphs
| `FS_BUFFER_MAX_CONFIG` | 65,536 B | Upper limit ceiling for configuration files
| `FS_PATH_MAX` | 512 B | Maximum supported filepath length
|===

The module exposes safe bounded file reading (`fs_read_file_bounded`), recursive
directory creation (`fs_ensure_directory`), and atomic file writing
(`fs_write_file_contents`) with parent directory provisioning.

=== 3.2 Zero-Heap JSON Engine (`core.util.json` Rev 2)

`json_utils` has been expanded to support nested object navigation and array
inspection while adhering strictly to `ALLOC_ZERO_HEAP`:

* `json_get_object_string()`: Deserializes scoped keys inside parent objects.
* `json_get_object_int()`: Deserializes scoped signed integers.
* `json_get_object_float()`: Deserializes scoped floating-point numbers.
* `json_get_object_bool()`: Deserializes scoped boolean flags.
* `json_find_array()`: Discovers `[ ... ]` boundaries with bracket balance.
* `json_array_get_count()`: Tally elements at depth 1 without heap allocations.
* `json_array_get_string()`: Extracts the *i*-th string element safely.

== 4. External Data Menus & Fallback Tripwires

Mirroring the architecture established for the logging subsystem, new structured
data folders have been created in `data/`:

=== 4.1 Mathematical Continuum (`data/math/`)

* `manifest.json`: Menu catalog registering active profile `default`.
* `default.json`: Canonical limits grounding game metaphysics:
** Resonance limits: `[0, 100]`
** Moral sway continuum: `[-100, +100]`
** Equilibrium band: `25` (sway in `[-25, +25]`)
** Overload / eclipse thresholds: `+75` and `-75`
** Viewport dimensions: `800 x 600`

=== 4.2 Timing & Frame Pacing (`data/time/`)

* `manifest.json`: Menu catalog registering `default` and `high_refresh`.
* `default.json`: 60 FPS profile (`16.666667 ms` frame budget, `50.0 us` turn budget).
* `high_refresh.json`: 120 FPS profile (`8.333333 ms` frame budget, `25.0 us` turn budget).

=== 4.3 Lexical & Buffer Disciplines (`data/string/`)

* `manifest.json`: Menu catalog registering `default`.
* `default.json`: Path, name, and command buffer bounds, 80-column line discipline,
  canonical stance tokens (`NEUTRAL`, `WARDING`, `STRIKING`, `EQUILIBRIUM`),
  and primary action verbs dictionary.

=== 4.4 The Fallback Tripwire Protocol

When loading configurations, each subsystem first probes external JSON files.
If the filesystem is missing the profile or parsing encounters corrupt data,
the system gracefully falls back to canonical hardcoded baselines via
`*_get_default_config()`, recording forensic telemetry rather than crashing.

== 5. Verification & Telemetry Results

All modifications underwent rigorous automated verification:

1. **Compilation**: Built with `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` yielding zero warnings.
2. **Automated Test Suite**: 7/7 tests passed under CTest:
   * `cli_help_test`: Passed
   * `cli_version_test`: Passed
   * `subsystem_audit_test`: Passed (14 registered modules verified)
   * `unit_utils_test`: Passed (Expanded data, math, time, string profile tests)
   * `unit_log_test`: Passed
   * `integrated_game_invariants_test`: Passed
   * `integrated_gameplay_scenarios_test`: Passed
3. **Master Compendium Compilation**: All 64 chapters compiled across AsciiDoc,
   GitHub Markdown, and publication PDF via `asciidoctor-pdf`.

== 6. Conclusion & Architectural Reflection

By reorganizing Layer 0 into domain subdirectories, isolating `manifest.h` and
`manifest.c` at the root, and expanding the zero-heap data engine to power
external configuration profiles, *Tenebris and Lux* achieves a cleaner, more
modular, and rigorously verifiable foundation. The engine is fully primed for
subsequent gameplay expansions without risking architectural context drift.

== Chapter 66: DevLog 011: Four-Tier Verification Strata & Ontological Testing Framework

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/11/architecture/devlog_011_four_tier_verification_strata_and_ontological_testing_framework.adoc`

[abstract]
== Executive Summary

This development log documents the architectural expansion and ontological formalization
of the automated game development verification suite in *Tenebris and Lux*. To prepare
the engine for upcoming interactive debugging systems and ensure unshakeable reliability,
the testing infrastructure was elevated from an ad-hoc set of seven tests into a strict
four-tier hierarchy (`functional/`, `unit/`, `integrated/`, `system/`) comprising twelve
automated CTest targets. Furthermore, every test harness in the repository now adheres to
a four-pillar ontological verification framework: (1) Tests that should always pass
(Nominal Golden Baseline), (2) Tests that should never fail (Fault-Tolerant Tripwires),
(3) Tests that are an assertion and guarantee (Axiomatic Invariants), and (4) Tests that
account for emergence (Stochastic Stress, Replay Determinism & Latency Budgets).

[quote, ProfessorSeanEX, CreativeWorkzStudio Architecture Council]
____
Before you can build an instrument to debug the machine, you must prove that each
individual cog turns true in isolation, that grouped assemblies mesh without friction,
and that the entire clockwork withstands chaotic turbulence. A test suite is not a
hurdle; it is the mathematical guarantee of reality.
____

== 1. Architectural Motivation & The Problem of Amorphous Testing

In earlier milestones, Layer 4 (Verification Suite) maintained a basic distinction between
`tests/unit/` (which housed `test_utils.c` and `test_log.c`) and `tests/integrated/`
(which housed `test_game_invariants.c` and `test_gameplay_scenarios.c`). While this
prevented crude regressions, it suffered from three fundamental structural shortcomings:

1. **Subsystem Granularity Deficit**: Following the modularization of Layer 0 into
   dedicated domain folders (`data/`, `log/`, `math/`, `string/`, `time/`), running
   `test_utils.c` tested all utilities simultaneously. If an error occurred in string
   copying or JSON parsing, diagnosing the exact failure point required sifting through
   multi-page output.
2. **Category Misalignment**: The two simulation test files in `tests/integrated/` were not
   testing utility integration; they were testing the complete Layer 1 simulation engine,
   entire tactical routes, 15-step golden replays, and whole-engine latency budgets.
   Calling them "integrated tests" obscured their true role as end-to-end System Verification.
3. **Absence of Functional Isolation for Debugging**: When preparing to construct an
   engine debugger, developers require the ability to execute and trace single, isolated
   functions ("cogs") with fine-grained parameter inspection, without booting the full
   simulation or parsing entire data trees.

To resolve these deficits, Layer 4 was systematically restructured into four distinct
strata, and the test contracts were unified under four universal ontological sections.

== 2. The Four-Tier Testing Strata

[source,text]
----
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   TIER 4: SYSTEM VERIFICATION                          │
│  tests/system/test_game_invariants.c & test_gameplay_scenarios.c       │
│  Full simulation invariants, golden replays & sub-50µs tick budgets    │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                 TIER 3: INTEGRATED UTILITIES HARNESS                   │
│  tests/integrated/test_utils.c                                         │
│  Cross-subsystem coordination, central manifest & multi-profile boot   │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                   TIER 2: SUBSYSTEM UNIT HARNESSES                     │
│  tests/unit/ (test_math, test_data, test_time, test_string, test_log)  │
│  1:1 subsystem domain verification, buffer sizing, and tripwires       │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                  TIER 1: FUNCTIONAL DEBUGGING HARNESS                  │
│  tests/functional/test_functional.c                                    │
│  Granular cog-by-cog execution with step-by-step trace telemetry       │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
----

=== Tier 1: Functional Debugging (`tests/functional/test_functional.c`)

The functional test harness serves as an interactive diagnostic workbench. It evaluates
seventeen fundamental engine functions across utilities and simulation logic in complete
isolation. Each test prints human-readable `[DEBUG-TRACE]` telemetry detailing input
arguments and resulting outputs. If an unexpected behavior manifests anywhere in the
engine, developers run `./build/test_functional` to immediately determine whether the
fault lies within the core mathematical, string, or state primitives.

=== Tier 2: Subsystem Unit Harnesses (`tests/unit/`)

Provides 1:1 parity with the Layer 0 domain directories:

* **`test_math.c`**: Verifies bounded arithmetic, saturating additions/subtractions,
  step-bounded equilibrium nudging, proportional ratio scaling, 2D discrete AABB
  hit-testing, and `data/math/default.json` profile deserialization.
* **`test_data.c`**: Verifies zero-heap filesystem I/O (`fs_utils.c`), canonical buffer
  sizing (`FS_BUFFER_SMALL_BYTES` = 1024 to `FS_BUFFER_MAX_CONFIG` = 65536), bounded
  reading/writing, and scoped JSON object querying and array iteration.
* **`test_time.c`**: Verifies monotonic millisecond/microsecond clocks, POSIX nanosleep
  frame pacing, ISO 8601 timestamp generation ("YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ"), and 60 FPS /
  120 FPS profile loading.
* **`test_string.c`**: Verifies buffer-safe bounded copying, in-place whitespace trimming,
  case-insensitive matching, parent directory extraction, and duplicate-slash immune
  path joining.
* **`test_log.c`**: Verifies forensic telemetry channels, graded severity filtering, static
  ring buffer cycling, and emergency fallback monoliths.

=== Tier 3: Integrated Utilities Harness (`tests/integrated/test_utils.c`)

Validates the multi-subsystem coordination of all Layer 0 utilities. Exercises the central
manifest registry (`manifest.c`), validates upstream DAG dependency resolution, enforces
diagnostic escalation policies (WARNING -> `PREFLIGHT_STATUS_WARNINGS`, CRITICAL ->
`PREFLIGHT_STATUS_DOWNGRADED`, FATAL -> `PREFLIGHT_STATUS_HALT_FATAL`), and benchmarks
multi-profile synchronous boot latency (< 5 milliseconds).

=== Tier 4: System Verification Harnesses (`tests/system/`)

* **`test_game_invariants.c`**: Validates Layer 1 simulation engine state invariants: twin
  relic power bounds ([0, 100]), moral sway continuum boundaries ([-100, +100]), protagonist
  name customization, and bidirectional provincial map topology.
* **`test_gameplay_scenarios.c`**: Simulates end-to-end tactical combat routes (Righteous
  Warden and Umbral Striker paths), 15-step golden deterministic replay parity, 1,000 chaotic
  command fuzzing for softlock immunity, and 10,000-turn simulation latency benchmarking
  (< 50 microseconds per turn).

== 3. The Four Ontological Testing Sections

Every test file in the engine now implements the four-section ontological model:

[cols="1,2,3", options="header"]
|===
| Section | Category | Mission & Verification Scope
| **Section 1** | **Always Pass**
| Valid inputs, nominal parameters, default configuration loading, standard state
initialization, well-formed JSON, normal gameplay actions. Proves the golden baseline.

| **Section 2** | **Never Fail**
| Inviolability under error. Injects NULL pointers, zero capacities, missing files, corrupted
JSON, and unknown stances. Proves tripwire activation and safe fallback without OS traps.

| **Section 3** | **Assertion & Guarantee**
| Axiomatic mathematical laws and structural contracts: bounded clamping, non-decreasing
monotonic clocks, strict null-termination under truncation, and deterministic stance completeness.

| **Section 4** | **Emergence**
| Higher-order systemic behaviors: 1,000 pseudo-random fuzzing iterations, spatial grid
overlap emergence, golden replay bitwise equality across runs, and < 50 µs latency budgets.
|===

== 4. Preparing for the Interactive Debugger

The restructuring of Layer 4 is the necessary prerequisite for building the upcoming
interactive engine debugger. Specifically:

1. **Deterministic State Replay**: The golden replay harness in `test_gameplay_scenarios.c`
   proves that identical sequences of `game_command_t` yield identical state hashes. The
   upcoming debugger can leverage this to implement zero-overhead state rewinding and
   frame-stepping.
2. **Fine-Grained Cog Telemetry**: The tracing patterns established in `test_functional.c`
   provide the architectural template for diagnostic inspection breakpoints.
3. **Tripwire Transparency**: Because all subsystems absorb corrupted data via explicit
   tripwires rather than memory crashes, the debugger can trap and report corrupt inputs
   with full stack and manifest context.

== Chapter 67: DevLog 012: Interactive Debugging System & State Rewind Architecture

*Source File*: `/home/professorseanex/Tenebris and Lux/docs/dev_logs/2026/11/architecture/devlog_012_interactive_debugging_system_and_state_rewind_architecture.adoc`

[abstract]
== Executive Summary

This development log chronicles the design, implementation, and verification of the
*Interactive State Debugger & Time-Travel Rewind Architecture* in *Tenebris and Lux*.
Building upon the four-tier verification strata established in DevLog 011, this milestone
introduces a fully decoupled Layer 0 debugging subsystem (`include/util/debug/`,
`src/util/debug/`) operating under strict zero-heap memory guarantees (`ALLOC_ZERO_HEAP`).
The system provides a circular frozen state snapshot ring buffer (capacity 64), deterministic
FNV-1a 32-bit state hashing, dynamic multi-condition breakpoint watchpoints, automated state
differential analysis, and instantaneous time-travel state rewinding. Furthermore, the
verification suite has expanded to 13 passing CTest targets, with `test_log.c` and
`test_debug.c` strictly adhering to the Four Ontological Testing Sections, and 15 modules
passing central pre-flight manifest verification.

[quote, ProfessorSeanEX, CreativeWorkzStudio Architecture Council]
____
True observability is not merely recording where the system failed; it is possessing
the mathematical power to freeze the universe, step backwards through time, inspect the
exact trajectory of causality, and verify that the invariants of balance remain unbroken.
____

== 1. Architectural Motivation & The Problem of Blind Mutation

In complex tactical TRPG simulations featuring moral sway continuums, dual-relic resonance
conservation, and branching multi-path endings, diagnosing subtle emergent phenomena
(such as state desynchronization, illegal stance shifts, or drift in moral alignment)
via standard print statements is inherently flawed:

1. **Destructive Mutation**: Once a turn completes, state is mutated in-place. If an
   invariant tripwire trips on turn 15, the precise historical trajectory that produced the
   anomaly is irretrievably lost.
2. **Layer Coupling Risks**: A debugger built directly inside Layer 1 risks coupling
   presentation, inspection, and simulation logic together, breaking the strict unidirectional
   strata of the engine.
3. **Dynamic Memory Vulnerability**: Conventional debuggers allocate heap memory for event
   queues and dynamic snapshots, introducing fragmentation and memory leaks that violate
   our embedded C11 zero-heap discipline (`ALLOC_ZERO_HEAP`).

To solve these challenges, we designed a zero-heap, decoupled state debugger in Layer 0
complemented by simulation adapters in Layer 1 and presentation controls in Layer 3.

== 2. Decoupled State Snapshot Ring Buffer Architecture

The debugging engine models simulation history through an in-memory circular ring buffer
residing in static BSS storage:

[source,text]
----
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│               LAYER 0: ZERO-HEAP STATE DEBUGGER ENGINE                 │
│  src/util/debug/debug.c <-> include/util/debug/debug.h                 │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  debug_state_fields_t: Decoupled Fundamental State Fields               │
│  ├── turn, hero_name, hp, max_hp, mp, tp                               │
│  ├── shield_power (Lux), sword_edge (Tenebris)                         │
│  ├── stance ID, moral_sway, location province ID                       │
│  └── threat_hp, foes_vanquished                                        │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Circular Ring Buffer (Capacity: 64 Snapshots)                         │
│  ├── snapshot_id (Monotonic 64-bit ID)                                 │
│  ├── timestamp_ms (Monotonic Millisecond Clock)                        │
│  ├── state (Decoupled Fields Copy)                                     │
│  ├── last_command & last_arg ("barrio", "cuttatio", "stance striking") │
│  ├── state_hash (FNV-1a 32-bit Checksum)                               │
│  └── log_sequence_id (Binding Snapshot to Forensic Telemetry Entry)    │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
----

=== Strict Decoupling Invariant

Layer 0 never includes Layer 1 simulation headers (`sim/sim_types.h` or `sim/game.h`).
Instead:
- `debug_state_fields_t` declares pure ISO C11 primitive fields.
- Layer 1 (`src/sim/game.c`) implements bidirectional projection adapters:
  * `game_state_to_debug_fields()` maps `player_state_t` into `debug_state_fields_t`.
  * `game_state_from_debug_fields()` reconstructs `player_state_t` from `debug_state_fields_t`.
  * `game_record_debug_snapshot()` captures turn-boundary snapshots.
  * `game_rewind_debug_snapshot()` safely rewinds the live player simulation.

== 3. Dynamic Breakpoints & Differential State Analysis

=== Multi-Condition Breakpoint Engine

Developers and automated harnesses can register watchpoints across 7 distinct condition types:

1. `DEBUG_BREAK_TURN`: Triggers upon reaching a specific turn count.
2. `DEBUG_BREAK_STANCE`: Triggers when combat stance shifts to a target stance ID.
3. `DEBUG_BREAK_SWAY_RANGE`: Triggers when absolute moral sway breaches a threshold.
4. `DEBUG_BREAK_RESONANCE_BREACH`: Triggers if Lux or Tenebris resonance exceeds limits.
5. `DEBUG_BREAK_HP_THRESHOLD`: Triggers when HP drops below a critical floor.
6. `DEBUG_BREAK_LOCATION`: Triggers upon journeying into a designated province.
7. `DEBUG_BREAK_TRIPWIRE`: Triggers when any subsystem trips a latched diagnostic wire.

=== State Differential Analysis (`debug_diff_snapshots`)

Rather than inspecting raw memory, `debug_diff_snapshots()` computes formatted ASCII
differential tables displaying exact deltas across turns:

[source,text]
----
Snapshot Diff: #1 (Turn 1) -> #2 (Turn 2)
--------------------------------------------------
  HP:           100 -> 90 (-10)
  Shield (Lux): 50 -> 45 (-5)
  Sword (Ten):  50 -> 55 (+5)
  Moral Sway:   0 -> 5 (+5)
  State Hash:   0xAA851EE1 -> 0xB79D3C20
----

== 4. Data-Driven Debugger Profiles & Fallback Monoliths

Following the data-driven architecture of `data/manifest.json`, the debugger catalog
is maintained under `data/debug/`:

* `data/debug/manifest.json`: Central catalog defining available profiles (`default`,
  `strict`, `trace`) and active default pointer.
* `data/debug/default.json`: Balanced profile (64 snapshots, tripwire traps enabled,
  1-turn snapshot intervals, interactive REPL active).
* `data/debug/strict.json`: Strict validation profile (break-on-error, break-on-stance-change,
  rigid state boundary verification).
* `data/debug/trace.json`: Fine-grained telemetry profile for deep engine debugging.
* `src/util/debug/debug_fallback.c`: Quarantined emergency fallback code page guaranteeing
  operational readiness even if external JSON assets are missing or corrupted.

== 5. Verification Telemetry & Verification Expansion

  - `unit_log_test`: Reorganized under the 4 Ontological Sections (2.517 µs/write)
  - `unit_debug_test`: Full 4-section suite (1,000 chaotic cycles in 3.125 ms, 3.125 µs/snap)
  - `integrated_utils_test`: Cross-utility DAG coordination and multi-profile boot
  - `system_game_invariants_test`: Invariant tripwires and relic conservation
